# P3 — The co-work layer (2026-10-12)

> **Parent:** `docs/charter.md`, the root spec; this document is its child at level **P3** (charter §3 "P2" paragraph's last sentence, "the co-work layer … is P3, the composite after P2"; §16's deferred list; P2 §9 "Deferred (may return; the P3 seed)" and "Owner requests received during P2 for P3", `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-08-p2-daily-driver.md`). Parent pin: `docs/charter.md` at master 5ad08789 (§3 as amended 2026-10-08, §19 through 2026-10-12). **Consumes:** P1's and P2's landed contracts X9 to X22 (charter §13) and their ledgers' names; the owner's vault notes `WorkStation Design/Claude Docs study — Enriching Side Panel.md` (2026-10-01), `Claude Artifact NOte.md` and `feedback.md` (2026-10-10); the owner's Claude Desktop report on the slide dialect (2026-10-12, Decision Log 6); the three research notes `research/p3-claude-artifacts-suite.md`, `research/p3-yjs-store.md`, `research/p3-editors-export-history.md` (2026-10-12, 5ad08789) and, when U1 lands, `research/p3-claude-type-formats.md`; the floating-card seed `docs/doperpowers/evidence/p3-seeds/2026-10-10-floating-session-card.txt`; the tracker's 77 P3-owned rows. Children dispatch per their grain hint and open by citing this document (path, child id, parent pin).

Status: **design and cut proposed to the owner 2026-10-12; revised the same day after one adversarial review round (Decision Log 20) and U1's probe of Claude's type files (Decision Log 21), the design system redefined on the owner's designer skill (Decision Log 22); **approved by the owner 2026-10-12 18:06 PDT as written at 60d7b4f8 ("스펙 승인", Decision Log 23)**; wave 0 dispatched from this approval (P3-0, P3-G, P3-H's spike; the ledger opened).** The owner's answers of 2026-10-12 are Decision Log 1 to 10 and 12 **(owner)**; the architect's are 11 and 13 onward **(architect)** and can be overturned. Intake shape: a coupled goal (one store, one agent protocol and one history model under four editors), so §4 is thick and binding where the joint view settled it. P2 stays open beside this document until the owner's declaration (P2 §12 entries 29, 30); the two phases share no file but the tracker and the ledgers' allocation table.

## 1. Purpose

**Higher purpose.** MAWS is a general workstation for supervising Claude Code agents, built to the baseline of the reference apps and then past it (charter §1, D-5, D-20). P0 reached the baseline, P1 moved the agent workflow out of the terminal, P2 made the daily surfaces at least as good as Claude Desktop's. What the owner still does elsewhere is the making of things with the agent that are not code: documents, slide decks, designs. Claude's Artifacts suite (Docs, Slides, Design, Design System, Dashboard) is where that happens today, in claude.ai's services.

**What this phase is for.** A self-hosted Google Docs and Google Slides for MAWS (the owner's words, Decision Log 2): the co-work layer the charter deferred since 2026-10-05, local-first, with the agent editing through MCP tools the way Claude's own tools edit Claude Docs and Claude Slides, the person editing in-app the way they edit in Google Docs, every change attributed to who made it and when, and a version history as good as Google's. It is built for the owner's daily work now and, later, as the base of a product for teachers (P4: sharing, simultaneous editing, a web-hosted MAWS), so its store and editors are designed to grow there without a rewrite.

**The measure (Decision Log 1).** Parity with Claude Desktop's Artifacts suite: on documents, decks, designs, design systems and dashboards, with their library, comments, versions and exports, MAWS does what Claude's artifacts do, and where the two differ MAWS is the one with a real history (diff, attribution, restore) and a real organiser. The owner's artifact work ends in MAWS.

**What it must not be.** Not claude.ai's services (P1 P-4 stands: nothing is stored in or fetched from Claude Docs; the formats are ours, shaped like Claude's so skills transfer where U1 confirms the shape). Not a plugin platform: the editors are the app's own code, not sandboxed type pages (Decision Log 16); only untrusted content (free HTML, embeds, artboards' support files) runs in frames. Not multi-user: sharing, simultaneous editing and a cloud MAWS are P4 (Decision Log 4), but nothing built here may foreclose them (§4.10). Not a redesign of the shell beyond the three shell items the owner asked for (§4.9). Not the browser's chrome (the cua session's).

## 2. Acceptance

P3 closes when, on the owner's Mac with the installed engine and a development-signed build, every item below is observed in one sitting (the agents' live runs under scratch config homes stand for what the owner does not run, P1 §15's rule), and the owner then declares the measure met after a week of daily use. Items are observable behaviour; the children's gates refine them.

1. **The library.** An Artifacts tab (main area, like Overview) lists every artifact across sessions: All, Pinned and By Space; a type filter with counts (Document, Presentation, Design, Design System, Dashboard, HTML, File); groups Today, Yesterday, the days of the last week, months, Older; search over titles and text; "Make something new" cards per kind; each row's title, kind, Space, project, last editor and time, the sessions that touched it; Pin, Rename, Move to Space, Duplicate, Export, Delete. A Space is a named folder the owner creates, optionally linked to a project directory. The artifacts the owner's sessions delivered before P3 are there as File and HTML artifacts with their sessions. With every window closed and the host detached, an agent's edits still persist, and the library is right again when a window opens.
2. **A document.** From a session, the agent creates a document and the owner opens it in the panel (or a main tab). It is a Google-Docs-like editor: click anywhere and type; headings, lists, task lists, tables with row and cell editing, code with highlighting, images, dividers, math, diagrams, date and mention chips; tabs within the document; find; the design system's look applied. The agent, asked in the session, replaces one sentence, inserts a section after a heading and edits one table cell, and only those blocks change on screen while the owner keeps typing elsewhere (Korean composition included); the agent's cursor and the blocks it is editing are visible while it works; the same document open in the panel and in a main tab shows one state.
3. **A comment.** The owner selects a passage, comments, and sends it to the agent: a thread pinned to that passage opens as a side thread (or goes to main) exactly as P2's card does; the pin survives the owner's later edits around it, a restart, the agent's rewrite of the sentence, and a change of the paragraph's type; the agent's reply appears in the thread; Resolve closes it and the mark disappears; resolved threads are listed and reopenable. The same on a slide: a comment pinned to an element follows it when it moves and shows as orphaned when it is deleted.
4. **History.** The artifact's version history lists automatic versions (at human idle, during activity, at the end of each agent turn that touched it) and named ones ("Save version" with a name and a what-changed note), grouped by day with runs folded, each row with its author chips; a version previews read-only with Show changes on: insertions in the author's colour, deletions struck through, "N changes" with ↑ ↓; it renders with the design system and assets of its time; Restore brings back the whole artifact's content (its tabs, comment marks and thread statuses and properties, or its slides and deck properties) as a new version and never destroys, and keeps every conversation posted since the version: replies stay, a thread whose passage is gone shows as orphaned (the owner's ruling, Decision Log 32); Compare takes any two; a "See N new changes" pill appears when an agent changed the artifact since the owner last looked and opens that comparison. Right-clicking a selection says who edited it and when, including edits older than the last compaction.
5. **A deck.** The agent builds a deck from a brief: deck.json and one slide file per slide in the dialect (§4.5). The owner opens it: a filmstrip, a fixed 16:9 canvas with zoom, every element selectable, movable, resizable and restyleable, text edited in place, snapping guides, z-order, grouping, add, delete and reorder slides, speaker notes, a presenter window. The owner moves a box and retitles a slide; the agent, asked to change a font deck-wide, fetches, edits and publishes; its publish of a slide the owner changed meanwhile is refused with the slide's name, and after a re-read succeeds; a publish of several files lands whole or not at all. History works per slide (changed slides marked in the filmstrip, changed elements outlined). Export gives a PPTX that opens in PowerPoint and Keynote with editable text and shapes in the right places (the deck's fonts named, or basic faces, or slides as pictures, the owner's choice), a PDF, a standalone HTML, and a Claude-compatible deck with tokens resolved. A deck written for Claude Slides (its own skill's examples) opens in MAWS with no slide held.
6. **A design system.** The owner picks one of the built-in defaults, or the agent extracts one from a codebase, a deck or a website (colours, type, spacing, components, logos, fonts) into a Design System artifact with a preview page; the owner edits a colour and the preview follows; a document and a deck set to it restyle; the default system per Space and for the owner is honoured by the agent without asking; the `ask` sheet can offer design systems as an input (P1 P-13).
7. **Design and Dashboard.** A design artifact holds artboards of any size on a pannable canvas in the same dialect and editor as slides, exported as PNG and PDF; a dashboard is a document whose chart and table blocks are bound to data files the agent wrote (and, if P3-H's spike proves the route, to a declared connector's tool, user-gated, refreshed on a schedule), each block showing its source. (Coarse by design: P3-E is cut at its dispatch.)
8. **The shell.** Dragging the session/panel divider past the panel's maximum turns the session into a card at the panel's bottom-left (transcript, composer, expand, close; a chord and a header button toggle it; × leaves a pill with an unread dot; over a Browser tab the card reserves a live column instead, per the owner's ruling); the panel header shows the open tool's title, its saved state and its actions instead of an empty bar; Mark-up mode draws on a browser page, a document or a slide and attaches the marked screenshot to the composer.
9. **The finish.** The tracker's P3-owned rows triaged and landed in waves; the scale review clean; the owner's sitting on a development-signed build covering items 1 to 8; the owner's open questions decided; then the declaration.

## 3. Grounding baseline (2026-10-12, master 5ad08789)

From the as-built map (the Explore read of 2026-10-12), the research notes and the adversarial review's checks:

- **Deliverables today.** Per-session JSON records under `<userData>/deliverables/<appSessionId>/<recordId>.json` with version copies beside them (X12, `src/shared/maws/deliverable.ts:14-37`), kinds `file | artifact | screenshot`, written only by the engine host, read by main with no cache and no index table; the panel tool `tools/deliverables/` with "This session" and "All sessions" (`listAll` walks the directory, newest first, capped at 200); viewer by MIME (`HtmlFrame` in a sandboxed `maws-deliverable://` origin with a CSP of `default-src 'none'`, `PdfFrame`, `Media`, else the read-only code body); no write-back from a frame, no export of any kind, `pptx` and `docx` handed to the OS.
- **Processes and storage today.** Main opens the SQLite index at app readiness (`src/main/index/index.ts`); the engine host keeps its durable state in its own files because it cannot reach main's database (charter §4.4; X17's observed-facts file is the precedent); in detached mode (E10) the host outlives the app over a JSON-lines socket (`src/main/hosts/detached-transport.ts`). The host's IPC exposes MCP status, reconnect and toggle, not a generic tool invocation (`src/engine-host/ipc.ts:539-565`); the recorded `mcp_message` protocol serves MAWS's own SDK server (`spikes/protocol-probe/findings-pass3-p1-extension.txt:138-146`). The panel tool context exposes `setTitle` only (`src/renderer/src/panel/tool.ts:20-35`).
- **Agent tools today.** The `maws` MCP server's seven tools: `send_file`, `ask`, `show_pane`, `artifact_create`, `artifact_update`, `artifact_list`, `artifact_screenshot` (`src/shared/maws/tools.ts:15-17`); artifacts are free HTML, capped at 16 MiB, read back only as a screenshot; a side thread resolves artifact ids over its ancestors.
- **Comments today.** `CardTarget` is `deliverable` or `browser`; an artifact anchor's locator is a positional CSS selector (X10); the card is in-memory, one per window; what survives a restart is the side thread and its lineage anchor; there is no stored comment entity, no pin list, no resolve (`src/renderer/src/side/card/store.ts`, `src/shared/anchor/anchor.ts`).
- **Editors today.** CodeMirror 6 for files with a merge view; Tiptap 3.31.3 with `@tiptap/pm` only in the composer (a minimal schema); markdown rendering through one `streamdown` wrapper; no WYSIWYG document editor, no yjs, y-prosemirror, y-protocols or prosemirror-markdown installed (`research/p3-yjs-store.md` §0).
- **Index and settings.** Index version 8, settings version 13, layout `regions` schema version 3; allocations in the P1 ledger's §4 table (X15).
- **Claude's suite** (`research/p3-claude-artifacts-suite.md`): every kind is a server-hosted type (editor page, `SKILL.md`, `format.md`) run in a sandboxed frame; decks are `project/deck.json` (v4: `title`, `order`, `sections`, `faces`, `designSystems`, `attachments`) plus one `<section id>` file per slide with notes in a trailing `<aside>`; designs `project/canvas.json` (v3) plus `.dc.html` artboards; design systems `design-system.json`, `tokens.json`, `README.md`, `components/<Name>/README.md`; publish carries `baseVersion`, keeps files not sent, merges when none of the sent files changed, and refuses with 409 naming the changed paths; human saves go into the same file set with per-file sha256; versions are whole snapshots with an author, previewed and duplicated, never diffed or restored; "Save version" takes a label and a note; the library has All, Pinned, Yours, Shared, Projects, date groups and a type filter; Docs is a separate store (project → tab → node; tools `read`, `create`, `batch`, `update`, `delete`). The slide dialect itself and the Docs formats are server-owned and not in any client (Decision Log 6).
- **U1, Claude's type files** (`research/p3-claude-type-formats.md`, read 2026-10-12 through the owner's Chrome, GET only; the 35 files kept outside the repository under `~/claude-desktop-re/artifact-types/2026-10-12/`): the slide dialect's closed CSS list is one generated table (`format.md:9-67`) richer than the owner's report (hsl and named colours, gradients, shadows, filters, transforms; bare numbers as px; `%` where listed; margin a no-op at 0) and it forbids `var()` and `currentcolor`; the elements add `li`, `br`, `u`, `hr`, `tr/th/td`; inline formatting is only b, i, u, an https link and a colour span; `data-transition` (fade, push, magic), `hidden`, `data-build-in/out`, `data-fit`, `data-group`, `data-crop`, `data-video*`; every other `class` or `data-*` is ignored, so element identity is `id`; limits (15 nested divs, 20,000 characters or 100 marks, 200 elements or 4,000 cells per slide, 500 slides, 512 files, 256 MB, 16 MB per call, 4 font families, notes 4,000 characters); an unreadable slide is held read-only (`slideHeld`) rather than trimmed; the editor re-saves normalized ("compare meaning, not bytes"); PPTX names faces, substitutes basic faces or exports pictures, never embeds; `deck.json` v4 has `v`, `createdOnFiles`, `lists`, `title`, `cover`, `order`, `sections`, `faces` (≤ 4), `designSystems` (≤ 4 records). Design artboards are whole `.dc.html` documents with `<x-dc>`, holes, `<sc-for>`/`<sc-if>` and a logic script, and may use the installed system's `var(--…)` tokens. A design system's `tokens.json` is list-shaped (`{family: {tokens: [{name, value, usage}]}}`, colour themes, a type block) and explicitly rejects DTCG maps. Docs: the type holds no content; the connector (`api.anthropic.com/v1/pages/mcp`, OAuth, not called) has eight tools (`read`, `create`, `batch`, `update`, `delete`, `query`, `export`, `guide`), markdown with `<?claude block k?>` markers bound to typed blocks, `pending` placeholders filled later, a per-tab `rev` on every read and write with `sinceRev` reads, and `answering: <comment id>` linking an edit to a comment. Dashboard: db documents (`dash/meta`, `dash/params`, `datasets/<id>` with `{kind: 'query', connector, tool, args}` or `{kind: 'file', url}` sources, `files/index.html`), a d3 page with `data-source`/`data-field` marks filled in `dash.onData`.
- **Yjs** (`research/p3-yjs-store.md`, seven Node spikes, and the review's own check): snapshot history renders through Tiptap 3's Collaboration today; the transaction origin attributes nothing, so authorship needs a per-update author log and one replica doc per writing agent; compaction erases update boundaries, so times need their own index; a schema without the `ychange` mark deletes live text when a snapshot is shown; y-prosemirror's `restore` meta restores one bound fragment, not the Y.Doc's other fragments and maps (the review's experiment: an old state restored the prose while a thread stayed resolved and a tab kept its new title); the demo's gc-off path is frozen upstream; a whole-block replace re-attributes the block and kills its anchors, and `updateYFragment` strips marks and re-creates intervening text; relative positions collapse on a paragraph split, so comments must be marks; a concurrent re-order in Y.XmlFragment duplicated an element, so slides need a Y.Map element model with fractional order and documents need a move rule; markdown round-trips byte-identically through `@tiptap/markdown` in Node; Yjs v14 is a release candidate with native attribution and suggestion mode but Tiptap's Collaboration cannot use it and migration is one-way.
- **Editors and export** (`research/p3-editors-export-history.md`): canvas frameworks cannot hold flex or grid and tldraw needs a paid key, so the slide editor is a DOM editor with its own transform layer (about 1,500 lines by PPTist's measure); PPTX through pptxgenjs with measured geometry in a hidden 1920×1080 window (1 CSS px = 0.5 pt under the wide layout), fonts embedded by post-processing, shrink-to-fit never written; DOCX through `docx` (comments, tracked changes, theme, fonts); PDF through `printToPDF` with margin boxes; the Google Docs history behaviour written as an eight-point spec; W3C DTCG 2025.10 tokens with an Office extension; Notion's `update_content` and Anthropic's `str_replace` as the prior art for quoted-text edits.
- **The designer skill** (`~/Developer/GitHub/designer/skills/designer/`, a Claude Code plugin of the owner's, 2.3.1): a design system is the project's committed visual law, the triad of a stance, a token file (any form; it owns values) and `DESIGN.md` (nine sections; it owns usage), found and adopted when present, derived through a sampler or a persona otherwise, checked by a QA protocol; `references/guidelines-authoring.md` and the two worked examples give the exact shape. Claude's `/design` and `/design-sync` mean a packaged React component library by the same phrase.
- **The tracker.** 102 rows: 77 P3-owned (eight themes, none about the co-work layer except L100, L120, L124), 20 the cua session's, 3 per Electron bump, L98 and 1 other with P2-F.

## 4. Design

Everything below is advisory inheritance unless marked **[binding]** with its joint-view reason. Children expand their own sections in place; a child that overturns advisory content records a dated line in §12 under its id.

**4.1 The artifact entity and the library** **[binding — every child reads and writes it, and it replaces X12's per-session records].** An artifact is a top-level entity independent of sessions (Decision Log 5): `{id, kind, title, spaceId?, projectId?, createdBy: Actor, createdAt, updatedAt, lastViewedAt, pinned, designSystemId?, linkedPath?}` with kinds `document | presentation | design | design_system | dashboard | html | file`. **The engine host owns the durable store** (Decision Log 20a): one SQLite database of its own, `<userData>/artifacts/store.sqlite` (WAL; tables `artifacts`, `spaces`, `artifact_sessions`, `artifact_updates`, `artifact_versions`, `provenance`, `actors`, `artifact_threads`, `artifact_assets`; its own schema version, a slot per X15), opened by the host alone, with bytes under `<userData>/artifacts/<id>/` (assets, version states, the file of a File artifact in place as today). Main holds only derived library state in its index (the list rows and the FTS text index), rebuilt from the host's `artifact.changed` events and reconciled by one host read at every connection, so the library is right after a detached edit, a host restart or an upgrade; main never opens the host's database. Sessions are linked, not owners: `artifact_sessions {artifactId, appSessionId, role: created | edited | viewed | commented, lastAt}`; a side thread's scope rule (X9's ancestor resolution) becomes "the artifacts its ancestors are linked to". A Space is a named folder, at most one per artifact, optionally linked to a project directory, in which case the Space's linked artifacts mirror to `<dir>/artifacts/` on every save as read-only exports (`.md` for a document, a deck folder for a presentation; Decision Log 8); `projectId` is a fact from the creating session, filterable, never a Space. Today's deliverables import at the cutover as `file` and `html` artifacts with their session links; the `deliverables.*` IPC and the panel tool are replaced by `artifacts.*` and an Artifacts tool (Decision Log 9: the word is Artifacts everywhere); `send_file` stays and creates a File artifact. The library is a main tab beside Overview (§2 item 1), and the panel's Artifacts tool is the session's own list with "All artifacts" one click away; an editor opens in the panel by default and in a main tab on demand (P1 A-2 carried), both replicas of one document (§4.2). Search is a local FTS index over titles and extracted text, maintained on save.

**4.2 The document store** **[binding on the model — the editors, the agent tools, history, attribution and P4's collaboration all stand on it; advisory on table layout].** Every artifact of kind document, presentation, design, design_system or dashboard is one Yjs `Y.Doc` (Decision Log 2, 11). The authoritative copy lives in the engine host's document service (`src/engine-host/documents/`), which already hosts the MCP tool server and outlives windows in detached mode (E10); the renderer's editors are replicas synced over one `MessageChannelMain` port per open document carrying y-protocols sync and awareness frames (the thin provider of `research/p3-yjs-store.md` §2; two windows or a panel and a main tab are two replicas of one host doc); each writing agent session edits through its own replica synced in-process by the same protocol, never straight into the host doc, so authorship is known per update. Persistence is the `artifact_updates` log (one row per update: `seq, actor, at, bytes`, appended under WAL with `synchronous=FULL` before the update is acknowledged or broadcast) compacted into `artifact_versions` states (full V2 states, gc on); **compaction keeps provenance** (Decision Log 20c): a `provenance` table of `(clientID, clock range) → (actor, at)` rows and the per-update delete sets, written as each update lands and never merged away, so "who edited this selection and when" and the author colours of a diff survive compaction and thinning (older rows bucket to the minute after 30 days). Versions are rows of `artifact_versions {seq, at, actor, kind: auto | turn | named | restore | import, label?, note?, summary?, enc: full | delta, bytes (zstd), designSystemRef: {id, versionSeq}?, assetRefs[]}`, each stored as a zstd V2 delta from the previous retained version with a full-state keyframe when the deltas would exceed the state, and one `artifact_head` checkpoint per artifact so a load is head plus tail (S7a, Decision Log 36: full states cost 178 MB and 4.4 GB of writes for one normal 200-page document over six months, the policy 9.2 MB); an update row is dropped once the head covers it and it is older than 7 days; only `auto` rows thin (all kept for 24 h, hourly to 7 days, daily to 30 days, weekly after), `turn`, `named`, `restore`, `import` and pinned versions kept; provenance rows merge losslessly into hour rows after 1 h and day rows after 30 days (`tier`); nightly maintenance on a database created with `auto_vacuum=INCREMENTAL`; the owner can delete versions older than a date; an `auto` version is also written when the owner views an artifact whose live revision moved since its last version, so "see N new changes" has a state to diff from after compaction (Decision Log 29); a retained version pins the design-system revision and the assets it referenced, and assets and design-system states are kept while any retained version references them (Decision Log 20g). History renders with v14's `DiffRenderer(prevDoc, nextDoc, {attributions})` over two throwaway gc-off docs loaded from version states; the editor enters through `configureYProsemirror({ytype: the version's fragment, renderer})` and leaves with `{ytype: live, renderer: null}`, the live doc never written; undo and redo refuse while the binding is not on the live doc, and a transaction filter refuses local changes in history (Decision Log 29, S3). **Restore is artifact-wide** (Decision Log 20b): one kind-agnostic host operation that reverts the changes since the version (S7a's `revertUpdateFixed` on a scratch gc-off doc holding the version and the live state, pushing the stack item directly, since v14's `Y.undoContentIds` drops a map attribute overwritten twice since the version and returns an undecodable 13 zero bytes when nothing changed; the overwrite-twice reproductions are regression tests; Decision Log 36), shipped as one update credited to the restorer, an `auto` version before and a `restore` version after, `restored` signalled to the replicas, every bound editor clearing its UndoManager on landing; per-kind adapters handle only the after-effects (the threads mirror, orphans, the id repair); a concurrent edit in content the restore keeps survives, and an agent batch whose targets a restore removed is rejected naming the blocks "removed by restore"; y-prosemirror's `restore` meta is not used (Decision Log 29 replaced Decision Log 20b's per-kind diff-apply: a name-paired structural diff grafts a concurrent edit into a replaced block). **The live revision** is the host's update sequence per artifact, returned by every read and every write; checkpoints are rows in the log, not the only reference points. The four `y-attributed-*` marks (insert, delete, format, attrs) on every node type and the `--attributed` variants of the strict containers (blockquote, listItem, taskItem, callout) are in every schema from day one, and schema parity between every writer and editor is an invariant checked in CI (one schema module, importable in Node, no React node views). Undo is Yjs's `UndoManager` scoped to the local binding and blocked while history is shown. The port provider holds inbound sync frames while the editor is composing (`holdRemote`, set by the binding on composition start and cleared when `view.composing` is false, a 5 s cap on the provider), awareness and ack frames passing, the buffered updates flushed as one merged update when the hold clears (Decision Log 33, 35; S4 and S7b). **Yjs v14 (Decision Log 25, S1's verdict 2026-10-12): `@y/y` 14.0.0-rc.28 and `@y/prosemirror` 2.0.0-15 pinned exactly, one `@y/y` forced by a pnpm override, the binding code in one module, S1's suite the gate for any re-pin, every document tagged with its binding version; provenance is v14's native attribution maps, persisted as one encoded `ContentMap` per update with times floored to the minute at write and merged by the host only when bucketing (Decision Log 29) (the custom clock-range rows of Decision Log 20c are superseded); suggestion mode is the agent's opt-in (`mode: 'suggest'` on a write, or an artifact set to "agent edits as suggestions"), direct edits the default (the owner's ruling, Decision Log 27); the verdict flips back to v13 only if S3, S4 or S7 finds a v14-only failure.** The detached host's transport gains a binary, length-prefixed channel for document frames (an X13 amendment through the ledger); until it lands, detached mode serves documents through the live host only, labelled. Awareness carries the human's and each agent's cursor and the blocks an agent is editing (§2 item 2).

**4.3 The agent's formats and tools** **[binding — one protocol shaped like Claude's so skills transfer, and one namespace the ledger owns].** The `maws` server gains one artifact namespace (names fixed in the P3 ledger before any sibling codes against them; the shapes here): `artifact_list` (the library, filters, a Space), `artifact_describe_type` (the kind's guide and format reference, printed on create as Claude Code prints a type's `format.md`), `artifact_create` (a kind, a title, initial content in that kind's format; the existing free-HTML create keeps its name and becomes kind `html`), `artifact_read` (a document as markdown with block ids, ranged; a deck as its files; versions by number; always returning the live revision), `artifact_edit` (documents: one atomic batch of block operations, §4.4), `artifact_publish` (file kinds: a files map with `null` removals, `baseRevision`, the three concurrency layers of Claude's protocol: the "you haven't read the latest revision" guard, a merge when no sent file changed, a refusal naming the changed files; `force` as the last resort; the whole map lands as one transaction or not at all), `artifact_upload_asset` and `artifact_list_assets`, `artifact_comments`, `artifact_reply`, `artifact_resolve`, `artifact_screenshot` (kept), `artifact_export`. **Every write is idempotent** (Decision Log 20d): the call's tool-use id is the operation's identity; the host records each applied batch or publish with its result and replays the result for a repeated id, so a crash between commit and reply never duplicates content. Each kind's guide (`SKILL.md`-shaped) and format reference are MAWS files under `src/artifacts/types/<kind>/`, versioned, returned by `artifact_describe_type` and attached to the creating turn, so the agent learns the dialect the way it learns Claude's. The default design system's guide is attached on create without asking (Claude's rule). Every tool call carries the session as the actor (§4.2) and ends the turn's edits as one `turn` version when the turn ends (§4.7).

**4.4 Documents** **[binding on the schema invariant, ids, comment marks and the edit tool's semantics; advisory on the block list].** A document is a Y.Doc with a `tabs` map (ordered; Claude's project → tab → node, Google Docs' tabs) and one `Y.XmlFragment` per tab bound through Tiptap 3's Collaboration; its schema (one module) has paragraph, heading 1 to 3, bullet, ordered and task lists, quote, code (shiki), table (row and cell ids), image (an asset), divider, callout, math (KaTeX, as the transcript renders it), diagram (mermaid), chart (a Dashboard block, bound to data), and inline marks bold, italic, underline, strike, code, link, highlight, `comment {threadId}` (overlapping allowed, non-inclusive; BlockNote's shape; the mark's attributes are its storage key on both Yjs versions, so the mark is frozen at one attribute and orphaned is derived: open, and no live text carries the mark; Decision Log 31) and the four `y-attributed-*` marks; chips are inline nodes: date, mention (a person, a session, an artifact), dropdown (a status). Every block carries a short id (`^` syntax in the agent's markdown, an 8-character nanoid in the doc; UniqueID keeps them through split and paste, the host repairs missing and duplicate ids after each applied update, and every writer ids what it writes). The agent reads GFM with `^id` markers and `<chip …/>` markers, ranged ("from `^h7`, 40 blocks"); a read may ask `sinceRevision` for only what changed (Claude Docs' `sinceRev`); it writes `artifact_edit(artifact, baseRevision, ops[])` with `replace_text {block?, old, new, all?}` (exact and unique, else an error listing candidate blocks; validated by its quote, not by its block's revision, so the human typing elsewhere in the paragraph does not reject an intact quote; Decision Log 34), `replace_block {id, markdown}`, `insert {after | before, markdown}`, `delete {id}`, `move {id, after}`, `set_cell {table, row, col, markdown}` (checking the table's row structure too), and two shapes borrowed from Claude Docs (Decision Log 21): a `pending {intent}` block the agent inserts as a placeholder and fills section by section (drawn as a labelled placeholder until filled), and `answering: <threadId>` on a batch, which posts the edit as the thread's answer. **The batch is atomic** (Decision Log 20d): the host validates every op's target against `baseRevision` first (only the targets, so the human's typing elsewhere never conflicts) and applies all or none; a rejection names the conflicting blocks with their current text; the response carries the new revision, one line per op and new ids. **The patch writer** (Decision Log 20e) applies every op on the agent's replica and never through `updateYFragment`: same-type text changes as a multi-hunk word diff of `Y.XmlText` operations with explicit attributes; a block whose type changes keeps its id and its marks, which are re-mapped onto the new text by the diff (a comment whose text is gone becomes an orphan with its stored quote); `move` re-inserts the block's node with its id and marks and records a move in provenance (authorship of the text unchanged), never re-inserting a block that holds a live human caret: the writer re-inserts the span the block jumps over instead, or rejects with "retry" when carets sit on both sides (re-inserting the composing block sent the following keystrokes into the neighbouring block, and ProseMirror rebuilds a composing block when its siblings move); the caret follows the id across a re-insert; the host reads incoming bytes before applying them and replays an edit that landed in a tombstoned, re-inserted block onto the live copy (otherwise it is garbage-collected on arrival); the host's id repair resolves a duplicate created by a concurrent move by keeping the later insertion after carrying the loser's edits into it and tombstoning the other, re-ids any other duplicate and never deletes, and detects a move per update (a delete and an insert of the same id) so the rule also covers a human drag racing a move; a batch dry-runs on a fresh-clientID clone, then applies as one transaction; validation against the renderer's exact schema with `createChecked` at every level (`check()` accepted a list item replaced by a heading); provenance applies the writer's carries, so a move or type change credits no characters to the agent, and history marks retyped and moved blocks (S4, Decision Log 34). Comments are the mark plus a `threads` map in the Y.Doc (threads sync, version and restore with the text) mirrored to `artifact_threads` for the library and the side-thread link; a thread is a nested node `{tabId, quote, anchor, status, messages}` with its messages as a sequence (one JSON value lost a resolve racing a reply), storing its quote and a relative-position pair for orphan display that the host refreshes from the live mark at its per-update hook when it drifted (it collapsed in 10 of 30 edits); resolve and reopen write only the threads map, and comment create, resolve and reply sit outside undo; a paste deduplicates a comment that already exists and a drag-move counts the source as gone (Decision Log 31); P2's card (X21) is the UI, with `CardTarget {kind: 'artifact', artifactId, threadId}` beside the existing two and the Side thread or Send to main routes unchanged; Resolve and reopen are new. The design system applies as a theme: the editor's CSS reads `--ds-*` tokens (§4.6) and the built-in default system is MAWS's own look (Decision Log 7). Import: Markdown and HTML into a document (B owns the schema, so B owns the importers); DOCX through a tolerant reader. Export: PDF (the print path), DOCX (an in-house serializer over `docx` mapping the design system to the Word theme and named styles, comments as Word comments, optionally the changes since a version as tracked changes), Markdown (GFM, ids stripped), HTML (standalone, tokens inlined).

**4.5 Presentations** **[binding on the dialect being the agent's interface, the Y.Map element model, the publish protocol, object anchors and the export route; advisory on the editor's internals].** The deck's files are Claude's shape so decks and skills transfer (verified against Claude's own type files, U1, Decision Log 21): `project/deck.json` (v4: `v`, `createdOnFiles`, `lists`, `title`, `cover`, `order`, `sections` with `start` or `before`, `faces` of at most four families as Google Fonts links or uploaded files, `designSystems` as a list of at most four records; an edit keeps every key it does not change) and `project/slides/<id>.html`, each exactly one `<section id>` (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}`, the file name) on a 1920×1080 canvas with 128 px margins, no `html`/`head`/`style`/`body`, inline styles only from **Claude's closed list adopted property for property** (`T/slides/artifact-type/reference/format.md:9-67`: lengths `Npx` or bare numbers, `%` where the table says; colours hex, `rgb[a]()`, `hsl[a]()`, named or `transparent`; flex and grid up to 24 tracks; `position:absolute` children and `position:relative` hosts; one background or a 2-to-8-stop gradient; borders, radius, shadows, opacity, transforms, filters, blend modes; margin, `box-sizing` and `grid-template` as no-ops; no classes, style blocks, z-index, em or `currentcolor`), the elements h1 to h3, p, ul, ol, li, br, hr, table (≤ 100×24, no spans, `tr/th/td`), the inline set b, i, u, an https `a` and a colour-only `span` (the slide-text schema is no wider), div, `img` (`alt`, `src` an asset path, `data-crop`, `data-video*`, a `src`-less placeholder), inline `svg` (≤ 52 KB, `aria-label`, no script or URLs), `<x-shape kind>`, `<x-icon name>` (the 30 names), `<x-connector x1 y1 x2 y2 head route from>`, `<x-embed>` (a pinned child of the section, sandboxed `allow-scripts` with no network, ≤ 16 KB, ≤ 8 per slide, flattened in PPTX and PDF), a trailing plain-text `<aside>` (≤ 4,000 characters) for notes; the attributes `data-transition` (fade, push, magic; magic move by equal `id`), `hidden`, `data-build-in` and `data-build-out`, `data-fit` (none, shrink to 60 % or 8 px then clip, grow), `data-group`; paint order is source order with all flow content as one layer; the limits 15 nested divs, 20,000 characters or 100 marks of text, 200 elements or 4,000 cells per slide, 500 slides, 512 files, 256 MB, 16 MB per publish. **Element identity is the `id` attribute** (Claude ignores unknown `data-*`; the Y.Map ids serialize as `id`, minted `e<n>-<hash>` when absent, and the PPTX `shapeName` is that id). **Loading** (Claude's rule): an unknown property or attribute is dropped with a warning to the agent; a slide that fails structurally is held read-only with its reasons (`slideHeld`), never trimmed. **Normalization**: the serializer is canonical and `serialize(parse(x))` is a fixed point; equality is by meaning, not bytes, as Claude says. **The owner's ruling (Decision Log 12):** the dialect additionally permits `var(--ds-*)` references for colours, fonts and spacing (a superset of Claude's rule), so a design-system change restyles a deck; export reads computed values and is unaffected; **because Claude's grammar forbids `var()`, the mirror and the "Claude-compatible deck" export resolve tokens to literal values** (Decision Log 21). Transitions, builds, hidden slides, video and crop are kept in the model and the editor; the presenter plays transitions and builds. Inside MAWS the live model is a Y.Doc: a `deck` map (title, sections, faces, design system, attachments), a `slides` map id → slide (`order` a fractional index, `notes` a Y.Text, `elements` a Y.Map id → element with one key per property, `style:*` keys, a fractional `z`, text as a nested Y.XmlFragment bound to a Tiptap instance on double-click); a canonical parser and serializer reach a fixed point after one pass, and a publish applies only the changed properties as the agent's transaction (`research/p3-yjs-store.md` §6). **Object anchors:** a comment on a slide or an artboard anchors to `{slideId, elementId}` (ids are stable in the model, so the pin follows moves and restyles); a deleted element's thread shows orphaned with the element's last text or a thumbnail; the `threads` map and X21's card serve both kinds. The editor is DOM over the dialect with MAWS's own transform layer: a handles overlay outside the scaled slide, drag, resize, rotate, marquee and multi-select, snapping to slide and sibling edges, centres and equal gaps, free (absolute) and in-flow (flex or grid children, moved by reordering, "Detach" to absolute) elements, groups as absolute divs, z-order as DOM order, text fit as `data-fit` (none, shrink, grow), one deck-level undo stack, a filmstrip (dnd-kit), speaker notes, a presenter window (current and next slide, notes, timer), layouts as design-system templates with placeholders, the Theme and Text style menus reading the design system. Export: PPTX by measured geometry in a hidden 1920×1080 window over pptxgenjs (`LAYOUT_WIDE`, 1 px = 0.5 pt, one-to-one mapping of text boxes with runs, shapes, pictures, SVG with PNG fallback, native tables and charts, notes; explicit sizes; three font variants as Claude offers, the deck's faces named, basic faces substituted, or slides as pictures, with embedding of uploaded fonts as a fourth where the licence allows; the 12 theme colours written by post-processing the zip; `shapeName` = the element's `id`); PDF by the print path (`@page 1920px 1080px`); standalone HTML. History per slide: a version's changed slides edged in the author's colour in the filmstrip, changed elements outlined, deleted ones ghosted, notes diffs below; restore per §4.2.

**4.6 Design systems** **[binding on the object's shape, the token format and the CSS names — documents, decks, designs, the exporters and the agent all read them; the owner's choice of definition, Decision Log 22].** A design system is the designer skill's object, not Claude's: *a project's committed visual law that new work must obey* (`~/Developer/GitHub/designer/skills/designer/SKILL.md` 2.3.1, `references/guidelines-authoring.md`), the triad of a stance, a token file and `DESIGN.md`, with a precedence chain (the brief per surface > a supplied reference, local > the design system, global > sampled ingredients > defaults) and a QA protocol that checks built work against its own law. Claude's design system is a packaged deliverable (a bundled component library with previews and a list-shaped `tokens.json`, U1); MAWS keeps a converter for transfer (Decision Log 21) but does not define the object that way. One directory per design system under its artifact: **`DESIGN.md`**, the law, authored (never generated) in the skill's nine sections (0 stance commitment with the ambiguity tiebreaker and `persona:` when one governs, 1 palette usage, 2 typography roles, 3 canvas, texture and material, 4 layout system, 5 component canon, 6 voice, 7 motion, 8 hard don'ts; about 300 lines; every rule checkable), which the agent reads before touching anything under the system and which wins for *usage*; **`tokens.tokens.json`**, the token file, which owns and wins for *values* (W3C DTCG 2025.10: semantic roles `color.bg | text | accent.*`, `type.title | subtitle | h1 to h3 | body | caption | quote`, `space.*`, `radius.*`, `shadow.*`, `$extensions["dev.maws.office"]` mapping the 12 Office theme slots and the major and minor fonts; the skill leaves the token file's form to the project, and this is the form MAWS's editors and exporters read); **the component canon** as files: `layouts/*.html` and `components/*.html`, slide-dialect snippets with placeholders, and `document.css`-shaped style roles for documents, each named in `DESIGN.md` §5; generated beside them, never edited: `tokens.css` (`--ds-*` properties and type classes), the Office theme mapping, the previews; `assets/` (logos, font files, which embedding and PDF need). The system is global authority for every artifact that references it: a document or deck carries `designSystemId`, restyles by token swap, and may override per the brief (the chain's first link); the agent's guide for the kind says so in the skill's words, and `DESIGN.md` is attached on create instead of a generated guide. **How one comes to exist:** (a) *adopted* from a project the skill classified as having a system (its tokens, components and `DESIGN.md` read, the values into the token file, "adopted from <system>" recorded; a CSS-variables file, a Tailwind preset or a React library all qualify), (b) *derived* for a brief by the skill's full workflow (the sampler or a persona, one stance, `DESIGN.md` before tokens), which is "building together" through the session and the `ask` sheet (P1 P-13's design-system input arrives here), (c) the three built-in defaults, each authored in this very shape (MAWS's own look is one, the document default), (d) *imported* from Claude's shape through the converter. When the designer skill is installed in the owner's engine it is the authoring path; MAWS's own format reference for the kind carries the shape and the rules so an engine without the skill can still read and write a system (the skill is the owner's plugin, not bundled; charter D-5). A design system is itself a versioned artifact (§4.2), so a document's or deck's retained version pins the system's revision it was seen with, while the live artifact follows the live system by reference (Decision Log 20g). A default per owner (settings) and per Space; the Design System artifact's page previews palette, type scale, the canon and layouts, edits token values in place (the token file), and opens `DESIGN.md` in the document editor for the law; a deck or document can be checked against its system's hard don'ts (the skill's QA step, mechanical where the rules are) from the header. Deleting a system that artifacts reference is refused until they are moved; deleting one that only retained versions reference keeps its states for history.

**4.7 Version history** **[binding on the behaviour — it is §2 item 4 and the differentiator].** Automatic versions at 2 minutes of human idle and every 10 minutes of activity, at the end of every agent turn that touched the artifact (authored by that session, summarised by the turn), and before any restore or import; named versions by "Save version" (name, what changed), any automatic one nameable; the list grouped by day with runs folded, author chips (the human, each session in its own colour), a one-line summary, a "Named only" filter; the read-only preview with Show changes on by default (inserts in the author's colour, deletions struck, block gutter marks, "N changes" ↑ ↓), rendered with the version's pinned design system and assets; Restore as a new version "Restored from …" (artifact-wide, §4.2), Make a copy beside it; Compare any two by shift-click; "See N new changes" when an agent changed the artifact since the owner last looked; "Show editors" on a selection from provenance. Authors come from the actor table (§4.2): the human, or a session by its title with "Claude" as the fallback. S3's refinements (Decision Log 29): "N changes" counts touching runs merged (102 per-keystroke runs read as 17 changes); comment marks are not changes; attribute marks on new nodes are not shown as attribute changes; restored content is credited to the restorer, as Google Docs does. S7a's storage policy (Decision Log 36) is what makes six months of history fit: delta-encoded versions behind a head checkpoint, the thinning schedule above, lossless provenance tiers, the library showing each artifact's history size, and the agent's writer writing a sentence whole when it changes at least half its words (B owns the threshold; marks on retained words re-applied), because word-level rewrites leave a tombstone per replaced word forever and put the heavy profile at 3.8× a fresh import.

**4.8 Export** (advisory). One export service in main, `artifact_export` for the agent and the library's Export for the owner: a hidden `BrowserWindow` on the `maws-capture` partition renders a document or a slide for PDF (`printToPDF`, margin boxes from tokens, `generateDocumentOutline`), the PPTX mapper (§4.5) and PNG; DOCX and Markdown are pure serializers. A LibreOffice render-and-compare test gates the PPTX mapper in CI where LibreOffice is present. PPTX import is deferred (§9).

**4.9 The shell** **[binding on the floating card's ruling and the editor host contract; advisory on the rest].** The floating session card is the owner's design of 2026-10-10 as recorded in the seed: a third panel state `floating` beside collapsed and maximized (a `regions` schema version 4, slot per X15), entered by dragging the divider past the panel's maximum, a chord and a Float button in the panel header, the card at the panel's bottom-left with the transcript, the composer, expand and close, resizable by its top and right edges, size remembered per tab, × leaving a pill with an unread dot, the docked request and tab notes kept in the card's stack; **over a Browser tab the card reserves a live left column instead of floating (the owner's ruling, binding).** The panel header bar (the owner's `feedback.md`, 2026-10-10: "bar above sidebar tabs are completely empty") shows the open tool's title and breadcrumb, an artifact's saved state (`saved | saving | unsaved | reconnecting | readonly`), its unresolved count, its version history, export and "Open in tab" actions, and the Float and Maximize buttons; **the editor host contract (X32)** is how a tool registers these (`{title, breadcrumb?, saved, unresolved, actions[], exports[], closeGuard}`), owned by G, consumed by every editor, the same in the panel and a main tab. Mark-up mode: a drawing overlay (pen, box, arrow, text) over a browser page, a document or a slide; Done captures the region with the marks as an image attached to the composer with the anchor's label; Escape discards; no change to the artifact.

**4.10 Web portability and the teachers product** **[binding — P4 must not rewrite the editors or the store].** The editors, the schema module, the dialect parser and serializer, the token tools, the history renderer and the kinds' guides live under `src/artifacts/` with no Electron, Node or `window.maws` import (a lint rule enforces it); the renderer adapts them through one `ArtifactHost` interface (open a document's provider, assets, export, awareness identity, connector calls when H proves them); the host's document service sits behind a `DocumentStorage` interface with the local SQLite backend now and a network backend later (AFFiNE's `DocStorage` shape), so P4 adds a WebSocket provider and connection-bound actors without touching the model.

**4.11 Versions.** Index and settings numbers, the host store's schema version and the layout `regions` version are slots in the P1 ledger's §4 table (X15), taken in landing order; P3 children that migrate: A (the host store's version 1), L (index: the derived library rows and FTS), G (regions 4), D (settings: the default design system).

**4.12 Delegated unknowns.** **S1 to S7 (done 2026-10-12, P3-0 landed at bcad5030):** every spike passed on Yjs v14 (Decision Log 25 to 37); S7a's and S7b's bounds tables in `spikes/p3-store/findings.txt` are the scale gate P3-B and P3-C must hold and P3-F re-runs (re-based 2026-10-19 by Decision Logs 108 and 109: C's drag row at p95 24 ms on the host-faithful path and three of B's rows; P3-F re-ran them as p3f-measure 39210430697). **U1 (done 2026-10-12):** Claude's type files read through the owner's Chrome (Decision Log 13, 21; `research/p3-claude-type-formats.md`); §4.5 and §4.6 are revised from them; what remains unread is the Docs `guide` topics (a tool call), the slide validator's code and the editor's own behaviour, none of which the design depends on. **S1 to S7:** the store spikes of `research/p3-yjs-store.md` §8 as amended by the review (Decision Log 20), run by P3-0; S1 is the v13/v14 gate (binding before any document is persisted), S2 the topology with the host killed mid-typing and main absent, S3 history on the real editor including artifact-wide restore of tabs, threads and deleted slides, S4 the patch writer against human typing including Korean IME composition, structural edits with comments and concurrent moves, S5 comment marks through every ordinary edit, S6 the slide model, S7 the scale gate (a 200-page document and a 100-slide deck, two windows and two agent replicas, typing latency bounded, first sync, history preview, memory and retained storage, IME, host-restart recovery), which blocks B and C. **U2 (done 2026-10-12, P3-H's spike, `spikes/p3-connectors/findings.txt`, landed with its live fixture):** yes, by two routes (Decision Log 24): route E, the engine's own `mcp_call` control request (no model turn, no tokens, every server the session has including claude.ai-hosted connectors, no engine permission check, a live engine only), and route D, MAWS as its own MCP client with its own per-server OAuth grant; the engine's tokens are never reused (a shared refresh rotates them out from under the engine).

## 5. Children

### P3-0 — Store spikes — spike (findings, never a merge)

- **Purpose:** prove the store before anything persists: S1 (v13 or v14), S2 (the host document service with its own database, the port provider, the detached channel, a 1 MB first sync, the host killed mid-typing, main absent then reconnecting), S6 (the Y.Map slide model with a minimal canvas, round trip, diff-apply, concurrent move), then S3 (history on the real editor with artifact-wide restore), S4 (the patch writer under human typing and Korean IME, structural edits with comments, concurrent moves), S5 (comment marks through every ordinary edit), S7 (the scale gate).
- **Acceptance:** `spikes/p3-store/findings.txt` with each spike's verdict and numbers; S1's verdict recorded in §12 as the binding version choice; S2's provider and S6's model as throwaway code the children may copy; S7's numbers against the bounds §4.12 names.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: approval. blocks: P3-A (S1, S2), P3-B (S3, S4, S5, S7), P3-C (S6, S7).
- **Contracts:** X24, X26, X27, X29 (evidence for their shapes).
- **Design inheritance:** §4.2, §4.4, §4.5, §4.12.
- **Required:** required (S1, S2, S6); S3 to S5 and S7 required before their consumers start.
- **Status:** in-flight (dispatched 2026-10-12, wave 0).

- **Acceptance:** a schema-agnostic document (Y.Map and Y.Text) created by the host's test writer syncs to two renderer replicas, persists across a restart and a host kill, is edited with every window closed in detached mode and listed right when a window opens, lists versions with authors and provenance that survive compaction, replays an idempotent write, and restores artifact-wide; a File artifact's bytes and a free-HTML artifact's publish behave as X12 did; the host store upgrades in place.
- **Edges:** blocked-by: P3-0 (S1, S2). blocks: P3-L, P3-B, P3-C, P3-D, P3-E, P3-H.

## 6. Cross-child contracts

Exact names are fixed in the P3 interface ledger (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-12-p3-interface-ledger.md`, opened at approval) before any sibling codes against them; the architect amends the charter's §13 at each landing.

- **X23 — the artifact entity** [owner P3-A; binds every child]. The entity, kinds, the host store's tables and schema version, the `artifacts.*` IPC and `artifact.changed` events, the Space, the session link, the `<userData>/artifacts/` layout, main's derived rows and their reconciliation, the migration of X12's records (L), the FTS index. X12's schema is superseded; `send_file` creates a File artifact.
- **X24 — the document service** [owner P3-A; consumers B, C, D, E]. One Y.Doc per artifact in the engine host; the port provider's frames (y-protocols sync and awareness over `MessageChannelMain`); the replica rule for agents; the update log, the live revision, version states, provenance and actors; artifact-wide restore with per-kind adapters; the binary channel on the detached transport (X13 amended); `ychange` in every schema; the `DocumentStorage` and `ArtifactHost` interfaces (§4.10).
- **X25 — the agent's artifact tools** [owner P3-A; B owns `artifact_edit` and the document read format, C the publish of deck files, H the bridge]. The namespace of §4.3, the guide and format reference per kind, `baseRevision` on every write, idempotency by tool-use id, the publish protocol's three layers, atomicity and messages, the batch's all-or-none rule, the actor on every call, the `turn` version at turn end. X9 amended; `artifact_create` keeps its name.
- **X26 — the document schema** [owner P3-B; consumers E, the exporters]. One schema module importable in Node; block ids and their syntax; the `comment` mark and the `threads` map; chips; the parity check; the document restore adapter.
- **X27 — the slide dialect and deck model** [owner P3-C; consumers E, the exporters]. The format reference (reconciled with U1), the canonical parser and serializer, `deck.json` v4-compatible, the Y.Map element model, object anchors `{slideId, elementId}`, the loader's warnings, the deck restore adapter, Decision Log 12's `var(--ds-*)` clause.
- **X28 — the design system** [owner P3-D; consumers B, C, E, the exporters, the kinds' guides]. The object's shape (`DESIGN.md` in the skill's nine sections, the DTCG token file and its roles, the canon files), the `dev.maws.office` extension, the `--ds-*` names and type classes, the apply-by-reference rule and the per-artifact override, the version pin's shape, the Claude-shape converter; names fixed at approval so B can theme before D lands.
- **X29 — the actor, provenance and the version record** [owner P3-A; consumers B, C, D, the history UI]. `Actor = {kind: 'human'} | {kind: 'session', appSessionId}` resolved to a name and a colour; the `provenance` table and its bucketing; `artifact_versions` row shape, kinds, the design-system and asset pins; the thinning policy and the retention rule for referenced states and assets.
- **X30 — the floating panel state** [owner P3-G; consumers the browser pane, the panel host]. `regions` version 4 with `panel.floating`, the keymap action, X5's host prop (the host sees floating as maximized), the browser pane's reserved column.
- **X31 — the export service** [owner P3-A; the mappers from B and C]. The hidden capture window, `printToPDF` options from tokens, the PPTX and DOCX entry points, PNG; `artifact_export` and the library's Export.
- **X32 — the editor host contract** [owner P3-G; consumers L, B, C, D, E]. `{title, breadcrumb?, saved: 'saved' | 'saving' | 'unsaved' | 'reconnecting' | 'readonly', unresolved, actions[], exports[], closeGuard}` registered by a tool with the panel host or a main tab, drawn by the header; one registration per editor instance; the existing tools register a title only.
- **X10 and X21 amendments** [through the ledger, P3-B and P3-C]: a third `CardTarget`, `{kind: 'artifact', artifactId, threadId}`; the mark-based anchor for documents and the object anchor for presentations and designs; Resolve and reopen; the browser and HTML kinds keep X10's anchors.
- **X15** stands: slots in landing order, the architect renumbers at each landing.

## 7. Ordering and dependency map

- **Wave 0, at approval:** P3-0 (S1, S2, S6 first; days, not weeks), P3-G beside it (no store dependency), P3-H's spike U2, P3-D's and X32's names into the ledger.
- **Wave 1:** P3-A after S1 and S2; P3-0 continues with S3 to S5 and S7 alongside.
- **Wave 2:** P3-L, P3-D and P3-H's brief (if proven) after A; P3-B and P3-C after A and S7 (B also after S3 to S5; C after S6 and D's first default). At most four workers at once (P1 A-13), the slot script, one e2e group at a time, the load rule unchanged.
- **Wave 3:** P3-E's decomposing run after B and C, with H's findings.
- **Wave 4:** P3-F after every required child.
- Forced: 0.S1 → A (the version choice before persistence); A → everything; S7 → B, C; D's names → B's theme; D's default → C; B, C → E; all → F. Version slots per §4.11. Landings follow P2's pattern: the throwaway-worktree master gate, the records, the push.

## 8. Risks and mitigations

- **The scale.** P3 is the largest phase: four editors, a store, a protocol, exporters. Mitigation: the spikes first and the S1 and S7 gates; the frontier rule (E is cut at its dispatch; B and C are reassessed at theirs; D's and H's scope is bounded); children land independently so the owner uses documents before decks exist.
- **Two processes, one truth.** The host owns the store and main owns the library's view. Mitigation: main never opens the host database; derived rows rebuild from events and one read at reconnect; A's acceptance includes main absent, reconnection and an upgrade.
- **The frozen snapshot path.** y-prosemirror 1.x's history has five "not planned" bugs (attribute changes invisible, code blocks). Mitigation: S3 measures them on the real schema; the history renderer is one module so v14's `DiffRenderer` can replace it; S1 may choose v14 outright.
- **Schema drift deletes content.** A binding that cannot build a node deletes it in the live doc. Mitigation: one schema module, the CI parity check, writers validate before applying, the host repairs ids after each update.
- **Human and agent in one block.** Mitigation: the patch writer's word-level hunks, the batch's target-only validation and all-or-none rule, idempotent writes, S4 with Korean IME composition under remote updates (the terminal's C1 lesson), structural edits with comments and concurrent moves as S4 and S5 gates, awareness showing the agent's blocks. Binding for P3-B after S4 (Decision Log 34): the composition hold on the port provider driven by the binding, and the caret following the block id across a re-insert; without them Korean composition fails 36 of 54 cases and 3 of 6 re-insert moves.
- **History that lies.** A restore that brings back prose but not threads, a diff coloured by a forgotten author, an old version drawn in today's colours. Mitigation: artifact-wide restore per kind (S3), provenance kept through compaction, the design-system and asset pins on retained versions.
- **PPTX fidelity.** PowerPoint wraps differently and never recomputes autofit. Mitigation: embedded fonts, explicit sizes, width slack, an optional "lock lines" mode, render-and-compare in CI, "Slides as pictures" as the fallback export.
- **The dialect drifts from Claude's.** Claude's list may change weekly. Mitigation: the list is adopted property for property with U1's record as the corpus; the loader's warnings name every dropped thing; held slides lose nothing; the format reference is attached on create; a token-using deck resolves to literals for Claude.
- **The connector bridge may have no route.** Mitigation: H is a spike first; E's dashboards bind to files regardless.
- **The detached host.** Documents need a binary channel the JSON-lines socket lacks. Mitigation: S2 builds it; until then detached mode serves documents through the live host only, labelled.
- **Scale on the owner's machine.** A 200-page document with two windows and agent replicas may make typing or engine supervision unusable while being correct. Mitigation: S7 with bounds, blocking B and C. S7b (Decision Log 37): held at 200 pages with two windows and two agents once the hold, its merged flush, touched-parent validation, the awareness fix and the idle unload apply; the residual is the patch writer's host-loop max, P3-B's 50 ms-per-batch check.
- **Storage growth.** Mitigation: gc on, version thinning with provenance kept, S7's soak with a published policy.
- **Portability drift.** An editor that imports Electron once is never web-hosted. Mitigation: `src/artifacts/` with a lint rule against Electron, Node and `window.maws` imports; connector calls through the adapter.
- **The owner's time.** Three sittings' worth of surfaces. Mitigation: F's sitting sheet is written as children land; documents can be sat on before decks exist.

## 9. Deferred and out of scope

**Deferred (may return; the P4 seed).** Sharing, simultaneous editing by other people, a cloud and web-hosted MAWS (P5's seed since 2026-10-19 by the owner's ruling that P4 is the smoothing phase; charter §3 and §19), the teachers product (Decision Log 4); Claude's other types (Sheets, Whiteboard, Animation, Tasks); PPTX import; sandboxed type pages as a plugin surface (Decision Log 16); Dashboard if the owner defers it at E's dispatch; live connector data if H's spike says no; the P1 and P2 deferrals the owner left deferred (Decision Log 10): the PR inbox, the routines editor, the prewarmed spare session, the sminos family chat, the credential store with autofill (moved 2026-10-12 to the cua board's composite #109 by PR #11; no longer P4's), realtime voice, the replay scrubber, remote control, plugin JavaScript modules, the Desktop-style eager warm.

**Deferred by P3-G's landing (2026-10-13, Decision Log 45).** Mark-up in main tabs (G's §H6; the main-tab header row is the recommended entry); E4a's cover acknowledgement as a full barrier (P3-F's row).

**Deferred by the owner (2026-10-12, Decision Log 44).** Design systems and components per school and per teacher, distributed to others; more default systems beyond the three; the sharing layer's work (P4).

**Deferred by the spikes (2026-10-12).** A composition hold scoped to frames that touch the composing block (Decision Log 34, 35; P4); keyframe thinning for heavy use (Decision Log 36); the patch writer in a `worker_thread` unless S7b's final demands it (Decision Log 35); `revertUpdateFixed`'s gap filed upstream as yjs/yjs#814 (the owner's ruling, Decision Log 38); its special handling drops at the re-pin that ships the fix.

**Explicitly out of scope (standing exclusions).** claude.ai's Docs, Artifacts and Cowork services (P1 P-4); patching the engine (D-1); MAWS writing under `~/.claude` or `~/.claude.json`; a MAWS plugin API beside `ui_surface_v1` (D-9); Windows and Linux (D-11); a second engine (D-7); the browser's top chrome (the cua session's).
**Deferred by P3-C's checkpoint 1 (2026-10-14, Decision Log 53).** Suggestions on decks: a presentation refuses `mode: 'suggest'` in P3; the per-slide accept and reject UI is P4's (a tracker row at C's landing).
