## 11. Surprises and discoveries

- 2026-10-12 (grounding): Claude's artifact kinds are server-hosted type pages; the slide dialect, the Docs formats and the PPTX writer are in no client. Found by reading Claude Desktop 2.16120.0, its web app and Claude Code 2.1.292 (`research/p3-claude-artifacts-suite.md`).
- 2026-10-12 (grounding): a Yjs transaction's origin is never persisted, so "the agent's transaction names the session" attributes nothing; authorship needs a per-update log and a replica per writer (`research/p3-yjs-store.md` §1, spike 2).
- 2026-10-12 (grounding): Claude's artifacts have no diff and no restore; Google Docs is the bar for history, not Claude.
- 2026-10-12 (grounding): the owner's `feedback.md` of 2026-10-10 (the empty panel header bar) had reached no plan or tracker row; found by reading the vault.
- 2026-10-12 (U1): Claude's slide grammar forbids `var()` while Claude Design's artboards may use the installed system's `var(--…)`; Claude's `tokens.json` rejects the W3C token format outright; Claude Docs' write syntax (markdown with typed block markers, `pending` placeholders, per-tab revisions, `answering` a comment) is close to §4.4's; a Dashboard is db documents and a d3 page, not files.
- 2026-10-12 (the adversarial review): y-prosemirror's `restore` meta restores one bound fragment only (the reviewer's experiment on the spike's dependencies: prose restored, a thread still resolved, a tab's new title kept); the engine host cannot reach main's index, so the first draft's "tables in the index" would have broken detached editing; no engine API calls a configured connector's tool outside a session.

## 12. Decision log

1. **2026-10-12 (owner), the measure.** Parity with Claude Desktop's Artifacts suite, over "the owner's artifact work ends in MAWS" and "the owner's three workflows". Rejected: the narrower measures, which would not reach the teachers product.
2. **2026-10-12 (owner), the storage model.** Claude Docs' model: a MAWS block store with block-level editing and the agent through MCP, "self-hosted Google Docs and Google Slides for MAWS", with Google Docs' file history (highlights, timestamps, who edited); ambitious, later the base of a product for teachers. Rejected: files on disk edited by the agent's own tools (the architect's first proposal; git-visible and cheap, but no block attribution, no history and no path to simultaneous editing); both at once (two sources of truth).
3. **2026-10-12 (owner), the order.** Documents, then Slides with the design system, then Design and Dashboard.
4. **2026-10-12 (owner), the add-ons.** The floating session card, the debt wave over the tracker's P3 rows, Mark-up drawing to the composer are in; sharing and simultaneous editing are P4 ("나중에 클라우드 기반 웹 MAWS에 필요하며 teacher들을 위한 제품에 필요하다").
5. **2026-10-12 (owner), ownership.** Artifacts are a top-level library with Spaces, not a session's or a project's. Rejected: per project (a teacher's documents without a code project would be "other"); per session (the status quo).
6. **2026-10-12 (owner), the slide model: evaluate both.** The owner's Claude Desktop report of the slide dialect (one `<section>` file per slide on 1920×1080, inline styles from a closed list, a fixed element set with `x-shape`, `x-icon`, `x-connector`, `x-embed`, notes in `<aside>`, everything else dropped, the Artifact tool's publish with fetch-before-edit refusal, assets uploaded once) against the architect's structured element model. **The architect's synthesis is Decision Log 14.**
7. **2026-10-12 (owner), the design system applies to documents too**, unlike Claude's one built-in look.
8. **2026-10-12 (owner), the disk mirror.** Only artifacts linked to a project mirror, read-only, on save. Rejected: no mirror (the agent's grep cannot find documents); two-way (two sources of truth).
9. **2026-10-12 (owner), the name.** Artifacts everywhere: the library and the panel; delivered files are the File kind inside it.
10. **2026-10-12 (owner), pull-ins.** Connectors for artifacts (P1 E2's deferred bridge) join P3; nothing else.
11. **2026-10-12 (architect), Yjs.** One Y.Doc per artifact, hosted in the engine host, Tiptap 3 Collaboration over `@tiptap/y-tiptap` on Yjs v13, versions as full states with gc on, authorship from a per-update log with one replica per writing agent, comments as marks, slides as a Y.Map model; S1 decides v14 before persistence because migration is one-way. Rejected: the demo's gc-off snapshot pattern (frozen upstream, loaders that forget gc off break history); transaction origins for authorship (never persisted); Tiptap's paid Snapshot, Compare and Comments (cloud-bound).
12. **2026-10-12 (owner), `var(--ds-*)` in the dialect.** Permitted: token references for colours, fonts and spacing, a superset of Claude's px-and-literal rule, so a design-system change restyles a deck; export reads computed values. Rejected: Claude's rule as is, which restyles by rewriting every slide file.
13. **2026-10-12 (owner), U1's route.** Read Claude's type files through the owner's logged-in Chrome (Default profile), GET only, no writes; "do all that you need to make this great". The probe ran 2026-10-12 (35 files of five types; GET only; the Docs connector's tool list from claude.ai's own server listing and its instructions from the web app's connector bootstrap stream, which may have made claude.ai reconnect the owner's connectors as a page load does; the owner was told); its record is `research/p3-claude-type-formats.md`, the files outside the repository.
14. **2026-10-12 (architect), the slide synthesis.** Claude's dialect and publish protocol are the agent's interface and the file format; the live model is a Y.Map element model; a publish applies only changed properties as the agent's transaction; the loader drops what the dialect forbids and tells the agent. Rejected: files as the live store (no per-element history, no concurrent editing); a structured JSON API for the agent (agents write HTML better than they call shape APIs, and Claude's skills would not transfer).
15. **2026-10-12 (architect), the slide editor is DOM over the dialect with MAWS's own transform layer.** Rejected: tldraw (a paid production key, a whiteboard store that cannot hold flex or grid), Polotno (commercial), Excalidraw, Fabric and Konva (no CSS layout or no ProseMirror text), react-moveable (unmaintained; its MIT math may be borrowed).
16. **2026-10-12 (architect), first-party editors, not sandboxed type pages.** Claude sandboxes server-delivered type code; MAWS's editors are the app's own and trusted. Untrusted content (free HTML, `<x-embed>`, artboards' support files) stays in frames. A type-page plugin surface may return in P4 for the teachers product.
17. **2026-10-12 (architect), PPTX by measured geometry over pptxgenjs**, DOCX by an in-house serializer over `docx`, PDF by `printToPDF`, Markdown by `@tiptap/markdown`. Rejected: dom-to-pptx as a dependency (generic heuristics for a closed dialect; its MIT font-embedding module is borrowed), Tiptap's paid exporters.
18. **2026-10-12 (architect), the history behaviour** is `research/p3-editors-export-history.md` §4's eight points, binding as §2 item 4.
19. **2026-10-12 (architect), the agent's document format**: GFM with `^id` markers and `<chip …/>`, ranged reads, one atomic `artifact_edit` batch with per-block conflicts, quoted-text replacement exact and unique (Notion's `update_content`, Anthropic's `str_replace`). Rejected: a JSON block API (token cost, Notion's own finding); Google Docs' index ranges (no stable ids).
20. **2026-10-12 (architect), the adversarial review round, all findings accepted.** (a) The engine host owns the durable store in its own database; main keeps derived rows only, rebuilt from events and reconciled at connection (the first draft put the tables in main's index, which the host cannot reach; charter §4.4). (b) Restore is artifact-wide, a host operation per kind diff-applying the old state, not y-prosemirror's `restore` meta (the reviewer showed it restores one fragment). (c) Provenance survives compaction: a `(clientID, clock range) → (actor, at)` table with delete sets, bucketed after 30 days (X29). (d) `artifact_edit` is all-or-none with target-only validation against a live revision; every write is idempotent by tool-use id; a publish's files land as one transaction. (e) The patch writer handles structure itself and never calls `updateYFragment`; marks re-map by the diff; `move` keeps ids and marks and records a move; the id repair's duplicate rule is S4's gate. (f) P3-H is a spike first (U2); dashboards bind to files regardless; connector calls go through the `ArtifactHost` adapter. (g) Retained versions pin the design-system revision and their assets; states and assets are kept while referenced; a referenced system cannot be deleted. (h) P3-A split into A (the store) and L (the library and cutover); A's acceptance is schema-agnostic; importers move to B; B and C are reassessed at dispatch against the gate. (i) X32, the editor host contract, owned by G. (j) S7 is a scale gate with bounds, blocking B and C. (k) Object anchors for slide and design comments in §4.5 and §2 item 3; transfer of Claude's decks is unverified until U1's corpus round-trips. Rejected: none of the findings; the reviewer's verdict "do not dispatch as written" is met by this revision.
21. **2026-10-12 (architect), U1's findings folded in.** Claude's closed CSS list and element set adopted property for property as the dialect's starting list, diverging only by decision (the owner's `var(--ds-*)`, resolved to literals for Claude); element identity by `id`; held slides instead of trimming; normalization as a fixed point instead of byte identity; the four font variants for PPTX with Claude's three as the baseline; `deck.json`'s real keys; transitions, builds, hidden slides, video and crop in the model; a Claude-shape importer and exporter for design tokens beside DTCG; `pending` blocks, `sinceRevision` reads and `answering` from Claude Docs; Claude's dashboard model as P3-E's advisory shape. Rejected: adopting Claude's list-shaped `tokens.json` as the source (proprietary and themeless beyond colour; DTCG has tooling and P4's web needs it); dropping the owner's token ruling (export resolves it).
22. **2026-10-12 (owner), the design system's definition.** The designer skill's object, "a project's committed visual law that new work must obey" (a stance, a token file, `DESIGN.md`, the precedence chain, the QA protocol), not Claude's packaged component library: "ours might be better fit for our design". Consequences (architect): `DESIGN.md` is authored law attached on create, never generated; the token file's concrete form in MAWS is the DTCG file; the canon is files the dialect can use; adopt and derive are the two routes, the skill the authoring path when installed and MAWS's format reference the floor without it (the skill stays the owner's plugin, D-5); the Claude-shape converter stays for transfer only. Rejected: generating `DESIGN.md` from tokens (a law written after the fact records accidents as decisions, the skill's own argument); bundling the skill (owner workflows arrive as plugins).23. **2026-10-12 (owner), approval.** The design and the cut approved as written at 60d7b4f8 ("스펙 승인") after the owner read the revised document; wave 0 dispatched the same evening: P3-0 (S1, S2, S6 first), P3-G, P3-H's spike U2; the P3 interface ledger opened with X23 to X32's names and the version slots; the charter's §3 gains the P3 paragraph and §19 the cut line. Children are pinned to this document's blob at the approval-records commit.
24. **2026-10-12 (architect), U2's verdict and P3-H's route.** The spike proved two routes and the brief takes both, E first: (E) the engine's `mcp_call` control request, declared in the Agent SDK's types and handled by 2.1.296 (`spikes/p3-connectors/fixtures/engine-mcp-call.jsonl`), runs a tool with no model turn, no tokens and no engine permission check, on every server the session has, claude.ai-hosted connectors included, only while that engine is live; (D) MAWS as its own MCP client (`@modelcontextprotocol/sdk`) for stdio, open HTTP and OAuth HTTP servers with MAWS's own grant per server (DCR now, CIMD in P4), tokens sealed with `safeStorage` in main. Rulings on the spike's questions: build E then D; an artifact whose own session is not live may use any live session in the same project directory (a Space's linked directory counts) whose `mcp_status` shows the server connected, never another project's, else the watch reads paused (X16 stands); MAWS's per-artifact, per-tool grant, stored in the host's artifact store beside the artifact and revoked from the editor header, is the only gate on route E, so main enforces it before writing the control request; route D's config is the one entry the engine reported in `mcp_status`, read-only, never a re-resolution of the engine's order; watches are the page's (30 s to 24 h, at most 64, paused while hidden, nothing while the app is closed); the engine-compat check probes the `mcp_call` subtype and the charter records the dependency (D-1 holds: a documented control request, no patch). P3-E's dashboards bind to files always, to a granted read-only tool through D, or through E while a live engine has the server; a model turn only on an owner gesture, which is also "Send to". Residue to the tracker (P3-F): `session.mcpStatus` forwards HTTP header values verbatim to the renderer (redact in the host; H's brief fixes it as it touches the IPC); headless engines approve project `.mcp.json` servers without per-server approval, a P1-era fact the trust card should state; a hosted CIMD client-metadata document (P4). Rejected: reusing the engine's OAuth tokens (forbidden, and the refresh rotation breaks the engine); re-resolving the engine's MCP configuration (managed > local > project > user > plugin, `--mcp-config` over all, claude.ai connectors last: too much to mirror faithfully).
25. **2026-10-12 (architect), S1's verdict: Yjs v14.** On the spike's evidence (`spikes/p3-store/findings.txt` S1, commit 9b99300c on P3-0's branch): both versions bind in Tiptap 3.31.3 with two editor replicas and an agent replica (v14 26/26 with a 54-line extension and 75 lines of schema hardening, no fork); history from two stored V2 states attributed from the per-update log meets 13/13 expectations under v14's `DiffRenderer` (author and time on every insert and delete, attribute, format and code-block changes shown) against 8/13 under v13's state-pair path (the code block vanishes, attribute and format changes invisible; 10/13 with a schema fix); suggestion mode for an agent edit 18/19; the markdown round trip byte-identical; v14 reads v13 bytes and v13 throws on v14 data. Ruled: v14, pinned exactly (`@y/y` 14.0.0-rc.28, `@y/prosemirror` 2.0.0-15, one `@y/y` by pnpm override), the binding isolated in one module, S1's suite kept as the re-pin gate, documents tagged with their binding version (a prerelease change to the mapping means normalizing stored documents, not an unreadable store); the verdict flips back to v13 only if S3, S4 or S7 finds a v14-only failure. Provenance becomes v14's native attribution maps (X24, X29 amended through the ledger). Suggestions are opt-in, direct edits the default, pending the owner's product call. Two pitfalls for P3-A's brief: UniqueID and the core Delete need `filterTransaction` under v14 or they write into remote blocks; an accepted suggestion must credit the suggester by clientID, not the accepting port. Rejected: v13 for its stability (its history path is frozen upstream and fails the differentiator's own acceptance); waiting for 14.0.0 final (no date; BlockNote 0.55 ships this stack on the same Tiptap version).
26. **2026-10-12 (architect), S2's and S6's verdicts and amendments, all accepted.** S2 (real Electron 44.4.5, P3-0's 38e62319): the topology holds (a utility-process host with its own `node:sqlite` store serving two renderer replicas over `MessageChannelMain` and an in-process agent replica; a 1 MB first sync 95 to 135 ms cold; keystroke to durable ack p95 2.7 ms with fsync and 7.7 ms with fullfsync; the host SIGKILLed mid-typing lost nothing acknowledged; the detached host took an agent's edits for 70 s with main absent and a new main relayed a renderer over a binary channel on E10's socket in 70 ms; fuzzed renderer bytes all rejected; provenance identical before and after compaction). §4.2 amended: an ack frame (type 100) and the rule that the host commits before revealing any update; `PRAGMA fullfsync=ON` (FULL alone does not flush the drive cache on macOS); every incoming update checked before it is applied and the document evicted if anything stays pending (plain Yjs parks a gapped or malformed update silently); a replica sends only step 1 and awareness until it has answered the host's step 1; log rows in V1 (the wire format), versions as V2 states; the live revision is the global log seq, rising, sparse per document, never reused; each clientID bound to its actor at first sight; main has one reopen path for host restart, reset and eviction. X13 amended: socket protocol 2 adds doc frames `0x00 | u32 length | u32 channel | payload` beside the JSON lines on one socket, `documents.open`, `documents.close`, `documents.closed {reason: host | reset}`, an 8 MB high-water reset. S6 (131 of 131 checks): the slide model is proven (a fixed point after one pass on Claude's own examples and on messy input; 13 structural failures held with reasons; `var(--ds-*)` kept and resolvable; a publish as one transaction, a no-op republish 0 bytes; 200 of 200 concurrent trials converged with no duplicates where the XmlFragment re-order duplicated in 200 of 200; 100 slides parse in 14 ms, 208 KB of V2 state). §4.5 amended: two paint-order keys, `order` for flow children and `z` for pinned children against a fixed flow-layer key (one key let a send-to-back backdrop rise over text when a replica inserted a new first flow child); a `parent` key per element with read rules (an orphan to the section, a cycle's smallest id to the section) and host-written repairs; minted ids seeded by the publish's tool-use id so a replay mints the same ids and a deleted id is never re-minted; **the publish diff is taken against the agent's base, not the live doc** (diffing against live wrote the agent's stale colour over the human's recolour), so X24's replica rule becomes: the agent's replica syncs at a read and a publish is applied as the change from that base; a `raw` key for svg and `x-embed` and `held` on the slide; border width and style on `x-connector` (Claude's own example uses them); table rows carry ids, cells stay positional; publish merging widened from "no sent file changed" to "no property changed on both sides", refusals naming the element and property beside the file; unknown inline tags hold the slide; only the host writes repairs. For P3-C's canvas and the export window: plain CSS paints every positioned element over flow content, so the under-layer rule needs emulation (z-index −1 inside an isolated section), read from the CSS specification and not yet painted. Both spikes are being ported to v14 and re-run; a v14-only failure flips Decision Log 25.
27. **2026-10-12 (owner), the agent's edits are direct by default; suggestions are opt-in.** The agent marks a write `mode: 'suggest'`, or the owner sets an artifact to "agent edits as suggestions"; then the edit arrives as a suggestion the owner accepts or rejects (Yjs v14's suggestion mode, credited to the suggester). Rejected: every agent edit a suggestion (slows the flow); a default per kind.
28. **2026-10-12 (architect), S6's v14 port: proven, A7 accepted, two single-copy constraints.** On P3-0's bb7e363d and baf47209 (`spikes/p3-store/findings.txt` S6): every experiment passes on v14 (142 checks: roundtrip 58, publish 19, concurrency 28 over 200 trials per scenario, canvas 18, binding 10, provenance 7, bench 2) and on v13; nothing fails on v14 alone, so Decision Log 25 stands. Concurrency is identical to v13 and update bytes are equal; an agent's added `<br>` now survives the human's concurrent typing in the same box (v13 lost the human's text); v14's attributions give per-element authorship with times (exactly the five changed elements, at property level, 4.2 ms, a 160-byte ContentMap), which is what §4.5's per-slide history needs; whole-deck operations are 1.5 to 1.8× slower, under 75 ms at 100 slides, and a replica's first sync 12 to 20 ms against 7 ms (S7 watches it at scale). §4.5 amended (A7): each text element's text is stored in `@y/prosemirror` 2's node shape (`p` and `li` blocks, `br` inline), so slide text and document text share one binding and one editing stack, and per-slide history reads element-level provenance from the attributions. Two constraints recorded on the ledger's X24 row: the binding module lives in `src/artifacts/yjs/` against exactly one `@y/y` (a binding imported from another package loads a second copy and trips the duplicate guard), and Tiptap and the binding share one `prosemirror-model` copy (two break the binding); A owns both pnpm overrides (`@y/y` 14.0.0-rc.28, `prosemirror-model` 1.25.12, the versions S6 ran with Tiptap 3.31.3) and a check that exactly one copy of each resolves; B, C, D and E import the binding only from that module. P3-C copies `s6/src/ydoc.ts` (the v14 seam) and the unchanged modules per the findings' Copy list.
29. **2026-10-12 (architect), S3's verdict: history and restore proven on v14; six amendments and five answers.** On P3-0's b933c193 and f3749834 (`spikes/p3-store/findings.txt` S3; real Tiptap 3.31.3 in happy-dom, host doc gc on): two stored V2 states render through `DiffRenderer` in the live editor with authors and times from per-update ContentMap provenance that stay right after a log compaction (19 of 19, including edits older than the compaction and attribute changes); "N changes" merges touching runs (102 runs read as 17) and ↑ ↓ visit all 17; Show editors matches the full-log truth after compaction (7 of 7); the preview renders 302 blocks in 33 to 41 ms and 1,202 blocks in 124 ms in happy-dom (S7 re-measures in Chromium); leaving history leaves the live doc byte-unchanged; the frozen 1.x bugs #82, #177 and #189 are fixed on v14, #128 cannot occur, #72 does not apply. Restore as revert-since-version (`Y.undoContentIds` on a scratch doc holding version and live, one update of the restorer) passes 30 of 30 document and 13 of 13 deck checks, covering a second tab, the threads map (a resolved thread back unresolved, a new thread and its mark gone), deleted and added slides and a moved element, a concurrent edit in kept content surviving, across compaction since it needs only states, 7 to 23 ms to compute on 25 sections and 0.3 ms to apply; a name-paired structural diff failed two checks by grafting a concurrent edit into a replaced block. Three gaps upstream does not handle, fixed in the spike's binding code and binding for B: undo and redo refuse unless the editor is bound to the live doc (otherwise history mode edits the live doc behind the preview); a transaction filter refuses local changes on the read-only preview (commands wrote into the throwaway version doc); every bound editor clears its UndoManager when a restore lands (undo resurrected a block the restore had brought back). Amendments accepted and folded into §4.2 and §4.7: the history renderer is `DiffRenderer` entered and left through `configureYProsemirror` (replacing state-pair mode and `unrenderSnapshot`); the four `y-attributed-*` marks (insert, delete, format, attrs) and the `--attributed` container variants replace `ychange`; provenance is one encoded ContentMap per update, merged only when bucketing, times floored to the minute at write (about 4× smaller); restore is one kind-agnostic revert with per-kind adapters for the after-effects only; both undo guards; "N changes" counts merged runs, comment marks are not changes, attribute marks on new nodes are not attribute changes. Answers: (1) revert-since-version is the one restore operation; (2) no "originally by" lineage now, the restorer is credited as Google Docs does; (3) the host rejects a session's next batch whose targets a restore removed, naming the blocks "removed by restore" (the batch already validates targets against `baseRevision`); (4) an `auto` version is written when the owner views an artifact whose live revision moved since its last version, so "see N new changes" has a state after compaction; (5) floor to the minute at write, not after 30 days. Accepted as is: one scratch clientID per restore in the state vector; the v14 rough edge that `applyDelta` with nested `setAttr` deltas throws, so deck writers convert deeply (S3's `materialize`). P3-A copies `s3/lib/restore.mjs` and `s3/lib/provenance.mjs`; P3-B copies `s3/lib/collab.mjs`, `s3/lib/history.mjs` and the schema additions (comment mark, callout, image, task list, the `--attributed` variants).
30. **2026-10-12 (architect), S2's v14 port: the topology holds on v14; the persisted attribution table accepted.** On P3-0's 386dd36a and 2706d2f1 (`spikes/p3-store/findings.txt` S2, real Electron): the host killed mid-typing lost 0 of 156 acknowledged keystrokes and both replicas converged; 120 of 120 garbage ports rejected; with main absent an agent wrote and the returning main's renderer synced in 69 ms over the socket relay; provenance stored as one ContentMap row per update in the same commit as its log row answers "who wrote it" through compaction, a restart and thinning (906 keystrokes: 46 KB of rows, 9.7 KB after compaction, 51 B after thinning; 15 µs and 53 B per update); nothing fails on v14 alone, Decision Log 25 stands. Three things v14 changes for the host, all absorbed in the spike's seam that A copies: a same-client gap is no longer parked but integrated behind a skip (the content visible and broadcast, a newcomer's sync copying the hole), while missing origins and unknown deletes are still parked silently and re-sent in every step 2, so the host's pre-check mirrors v14's `getMissing` and refuses every gap (of 2,000 mutated updates, 374 would have left parked state or a hole; the check refused every non-clean one); client ids are 53-bit, so X13's doc-frame header keeps its u32 channel and a Yjs clientId never goes in a frame header; cold first sync is 10 to 20% slower (5 MB in 444 to 487 ms against 390 to 424) with a lower host heap, reload after compaction 70 to 75 ms against 28, keystroke ack latency unchanged (p95 2.2 ms with fsync, 7.7 ms with fullfsync; a 48.9 ms outlier under a load average of 39 was disk contention and did not repeat). Persisted shape accepted: `artifact_attributions {artifactId, fromSeq, toSeq, kind: update | merged, bucketMs, bytes}`, one `update` row per log row in the same commit, compaction at version boundaries merging the folded range into one `merged` row (so each merged row is exactly one version's changes and "which version or turn" stays answerable), thinning re-bucketing and merging adjacent rows, rows never deleted while a retained version needs them; with Decision Log 29's floor-to-the-minute at write, `bucketMs` is 60,000 from the first row and thinning only merges. S7b (Electron at scale: typing p95 on a 200-page document with two windows and two agents, first sync, memory, IME under load, host restart) starts when S4 lands so it can drive S4's typing and IME harness. P3-A copies `s2/v14/*` and the Yjs-free `s2/src/{frames, host/ports, socket/*}` per the findings' Copy list; the Electron harness is not copied.
31. **2026-10-12 (architect), S5's verdict: the comment design holds on v14; six amendments accepted, one provisional pending the owner; pnpm overrides move to the workspace file.** On P3-0's ae103f33 and 2d0b0427 (`spikes/p3-store/findings.txt` S5; 211 of 211 pin checks, 10 of 10 read-format checks, 200 of 200 concurrent trials): a comment's pin survives every ordinary edit on both replicas and undo restores it (typing around and inside, Enter mid-passage and before it, joins, type changes to heading, list item and quote, cut-paste moving the comment, copy-paste and foreign paste deduplicated as Outline does, agent rewrites keeping the mark on unchanged words and orphaning a thread only when all its words go, a restart and a restore); orphan display works from the stored quote plus `locate`; the agent's read format carries `<c:ID>…</c:ID>` milestones in the GFM so overlapping comments need no nesting, with a footer listing open threads and orphans, exercised through `artifact_comments`, `artifact_reply`, `artifact_resolve` and `artifact_edit` with `answering`. §4.4 amended in place: (A1) the mark is frozen as `comment {threadId}`, `orphan` dropped and derived (the mark's attributes are its storage key on both versions, so a stored flag or a later attribute re-keys every comment, and two schema versions would re-key each other's back and forth); (A2) a thread is a nested node with its messages as a sequence and carries `tabId` (a resolve racing a reply lost one of two writes to one JSON value); (A3) resolve and reopen write only the threads map, and comment create, resolve and reply sit outside Ctrl+Z (flagging the mark would rewrite formatting and put every resolve into history); (A4) paste deduplicates, a drag-move counts the source as gone; (A5) the patch writer merges word-diff hunks across whitespace, otherwise a rewritten sentence leaves comments on stray new words, to be reconciled with S4's mark rule when S4 lands; (A6) the host refreshes a drifted anchor from the live mark at its per-update hook beside the id repair, all extents in one walk (4 ms for 300 threads, 270 to 510 B, only when drifted). (A7, confirmed by the owner in Decision Log 32) restore reverts text, marks and thread statuses but keeps the conversations posted since the version, as Google Docs keeps comments across a restore (`restoreKeepingConversation`, one 715 B update, tested); §2 item 4 says threads are restored, so the owner is asked and the entry is confirmed or reversed on the answer. Answers: (2) when the agent replaces exactly the commented words the pin stays on the new words, `answering` posting the edit into the thread (§2 item 3); (3) host refresh, not a version lookup at display time; (4) comment markers in the agent's `new` text are ignored for now. For P3-B: allow `comment` on codeBlock (retyping a passage to code orphans its comments today); write the thread record before the mark, or the host strips marks without a thread; use `tr.addMark`, not Tiptap's `setMark`, which copies an overlapping comment's attributes into the new one. Correction to Decision Log 28: pnpm 11 ignores `pnpm.overrides` inside a package.json (the spikes' overrides did nothing; exact pins deduplicated), so A's `@y/y` and `prosemirror-model` overrides go in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `overrides:` beside the existing highlight.js one, the one-copy check kept.
32. **2026-10-12 (owner), restore keeps conversations.** Asked whether a restore should take back the replies and new threads posted since the version (S3's revert does, as §2 item 4 read) or revert only text, marks and thread statuses and keep the conversations (S5's `restoreKeepingConversation`, as Google Docs keeps comments across a restore), the owner chose to keep them: "대화는 남긴다: 텍스트·마크·해결 상태만 되돌리고, 이후 달린 답글과 새 스레드는 유지(본문이 사라진 스레드는 '고아'로 표시)". §2 item 4 amended in place; Decision Log 31's A7 confirmed; one restore behaviour for every kind, so A's `keepConversations` flag is dropped.
33. **2026-10-12 (architect), S4's interim finding: remote frames are held during IME composition; A5 reconciled in S5's favour.** S4 (P3-0, verdict pending) reproduced on v13 and v14 alike that a remote update touching the text node being composed, or making ProseMirror rebuild the composing block (siblings moved around it), ends Korean composition early and doubles jamo or syllables ("세요요", "하셍세요"); not v14-specific, so Decision Log 25 stands. The fix, verified 54 of 54 composition cases against 48 of 54 without it, holds incoming remote frames while the editor is composing and applies them at the next moment it is not (holds of 8 to 240 ms). Placement, binding on §4.2 and X24: the port provider buffers inbound sync frames (type 0) while a hold is set and never drops one; awareness (1) and ack (100) frames pass, since neither touches the document; the editor binding sets the hold on composition start and clears it when `view.composing` is false again, with a 5 s cap so a stuck composition cannot part a replica indefinitely; S7b reports hold durations and how far the held replica falls behind and catches up. A5 reconciled: S5's rule wins and S4's writer implements it (S5's five agent-rewrite cases through `replace_block`, 6 of 6); S4's earlier rule orphaned a comment when the agent reworded its passage in place, breaking §2 item 3. The writer keeps one superset: on unchanged words the formatting markdown can express (bold, italic, strike, underline, code, link) follows the agent's markdown, so the agent can unbold, and comments are kept; the writer emits `comment {threadId}` only, never writes the threads map, and reports orphaned threads in the batch result as derived. P2's C1 (Korean IME doubling in the composer, tracker L98, not reproduced) has the same shape; this mechanism is recorded in the P2 spec as a lead for F's final landing, not as a fix.
34. **2026-10-12 (architect), S4's verdict: the patch writer works on v14 and Korean composition survives the agent's edits with four additions; S1 strengthened.** On P3-0's b93e492e and 5717ea53 (`spikes/p3-store/findings.txt` S4; 68 of 68 composition cases in hidden Electron, writer 41 of 41, moves 12 of 12). Without the hold v14 passes 36 of 54 and v13 32 of 54, the doubling identical; with the hold v13 passes only 46 of 54 because a held update applied between syllables moves the just-committed syllable to the end of the paragraph (y-tiptap rebuilds the whole doc and restores the selection from relative positions; v14 maps deltas to steps), so Decision Log 25 stands strengthened. The four additions, all binding: (1) the composition hold in Decision Log 33's split (226 of 226 frames applied in order, 288 agent awareness pings mid-composition ended no composition, the longest hold 447 ms, the cap never fired unforced; forced, it reproduces the doubled jamo once); (2) the move rule: never re-insert the block holding a live human caret, re-insert the span it jumps over, or reject with "retry" when carets sit on both sides; (3) the caret follows the id across a re-insert (3 of 6 re-insert runs failed without it); (4) host forwarding: an edit that lands in a tombstoned, re-inserted block is garbage-collected on arrival, so the host reads incoming bytes before applying them and replays those edits onto the live copy. Writer facts: all-or-none by a dry run on a fresh-clientID clone then one transaction; `createChecked` at every level; idempotent replay; concurrent moves repaired by keeping the later insertion after carrying the loser's edits (without the carry the human's edit in the losing copy is lost); about 28 ms p50 and 45 ms p95 per edit at 320 blocks, 22 ms of it validating the whole document twice; attribution after a word-level batch covers only the changed words. §4.4 amended in place: `replace_text` validates by its quote, `set_cell` checks the row structure, the move rule, forwarding, keep-later-after-carry with other duplicates re-id'd and never deleted, move detection per update, the dry run, provenance applying the carries, history marking retyped and moved blocks; §8's "Human and agent in one block" names the hold and caret-follows-id as binding for P3-B. Answers: (1) the 5 s cap stays as the backstop and flushes when it fires, with no deferral to the next composition end (a deferral would leave a stuck composition unbounded, which is what the cap exists for), and every firing is logged; (2) the hold delaying every remote frame by up to about 450 ms is accepted for P3, narrowing to frames that touch the composing block deferred to P4 (§9); (3) a human drag carries ids but no carry, so the host detects moves per update and keep-later applies to a drag racing a move; (4) P3-B validates only the touched parents, advisory until S7b's 200-page measurement. S7a (storage soak) and S7b (Electron scale with S4's composition drive and the hold) remain.
35. **2026-10-12 (architect), S7b's interim: touched-parent validation becomes binding; the hold flushes as one merged update.** S7b (P3-0, final pending on a quiet machine; a 200-page document, two agents writing, the owner typing) found that only the patch writer's batches stall the host: with whole-document validation `replace_text` p95 is 180 to 206 ms, the host event-loop delay p99 125 ms and max 212 ms, the typist's ack p95 142 ms and the second window seeing the typing at p95 131 ms; with touched-parent validation 66 to 98 ms, 41 ms, 51 ms and 41 ms; the renderer's frame p95 is unaffected either way (13.6 and 11.9 ms). Decision Log 34's answer (4) is now binding: P3-B's writer validates only the touched parents. Advisory for B, in this order: index block ids instead of walking the tree per op; replace the per-batch whole-document clone (27 to 36 ms p50, up to 88 ms) with the agent's replica as the sandbox, since Decision Log 26 already syncs it from the host only at a read, applying the batch there, shipping the update only when every op validated and reloading the replica from its base on a failure, which keeps the all-or-none rule and the invariant that no partial update ever leaves the writer; a writer in a `worker_thread` is deferred unless S7b's final shows the host loop's p99 above 50 ms with touched-parent validation. IME under load with S4's composition drive: without the hold 3 of 3 corrupted on v14, with it 3 of 3 exact and the cap never fired; a hold lasts 1.1 to 1.3 s (one Korean word, since commit-and-restart keeps `view.composing` true across syllables) and buffers 7 to 9 frames (5 to 12 KB); flushing them one by one costs 63 to 103 ms and leaves a long task after each word, merging them with `Y.mergeUpdates` into one update 21 to 27 ms and none. §4.2 and X24 amended: the provider flushes a hold's buffered updates as one merged update. Remote edits to other blocks appearing about a second late while the owner types Korean stays within Decision Log 34's acceptance of the whole-frame hold and strengthens the P4 deferral of a block-scoped hold (§9).
36. **2026-10-12 (architect), S7a's verdict: the storage policy replaces full-state versions; a v14-only revert gap, fixed in three lines, does not flip S1.** On P3-0's 72f14737 and 6eb03f99 (`spikes/p3-store/findings.txt` S7a, a six-month soak on four stores). The gap: v14's `Y.undoContentIds`, under S3's revert, builds its stack item as inserts minus deletes and deletes minus inserts, so a value inserted and deleted since the version lands in neither set and the redo reads it as a concurrent change, leaving the key deleted; S3's revert left 9 of 83 slide files wrong for a one-day-old deck version and 49 of 83 for a 30-day-old one, and a heading level changed twice, a task's checked state, a callout's tone and a code block's language all came back wrong. `revertUpdateFixed` (`s7/soak/revert.mjs`) pushes the stack item directly, every item inserted since plus redo of deletes minus inserts only, exact at every age on all four stores; S3's revert also returned 13 zero bytes when nothing changed, which is not a decodable update, and the fix returns an empty document's update. Decision Log 25 stands: v13 has no artifact-wide revert primitive at all and S3's id-paired structural diff was exact on every store as a fallback. §4.2 amended: A copies `revertUpdateFixed`, not S3's `revertUpdate`, with the overwrite-twice reproductions as regression tests. Filing the gap upstream is the owner's call (outward-facing); recommended. The spec as written fails the bounds: full V2 states per version cost 178 MB and 4.4 GB of writes for one normal 200-page document over six months (a deck 31 MB). The policy passes the normal profile (document 9.2 MB, deck 2.1 MB, load 133 ms, restore 779 ms exact, Show editors 100% after thinning) with one marginal miss (the history preview of a three-month-old version 1.08 to 1.30 s against 1 s on a document grown to 136k words; measured under load 9 to 20) and fails the heavy profile on time (word-level agent rewrites leave a tombstone per replaced word forever: live state 3.8× a fresh import, load 796 ms, preview 6.8 s, restore 3.7 s; a sentence-level writer cures most of it, the preview still 1.6 s). Policy adopted, binding on §4.2, §4.7 and X29: versions as zstd V2 deltas from the previous retained version with a full keyframe when deltas would exceed the state, one `artifact_head` checkpoint per artifact (load = head + tail), the version row gaining `enc: full | delta` and zstd `bytes`; an update row dropped once the head covers it and it is older than 7 days; only `auto` versions thin (all for 24 h, hourly to 7 days, daily to 30 days, weekly after), `turn`, `named`, `restore`, `import` and pinned kept; provenance one ContentMap per update merged losslessly into hour rows after 1 h and day rows after 30 days (`tier: update | hour | day` with from and to times), the merged map kept in memory and the version pair held open while browsing so the preview comes under 1 s (S7b measures the editor path); nightly maintenance, `auto_vacuum=INCREMENTAL`; a "Delete versions older than…" owner action; the writer writes a sentence whole when it changes at least half its words (B owns the threshold, re-applying marks on retained words). Answers: (1) deltas with a head, yes; (2) turn versions kept, +1.7 MB per normal document over six months; (3) the sentence rule, yes; (4) as above; (5) zstd, yes, `node:zlib` has it in the host (A confirms inside Electron 44.4.5); (6) heavy-use keyframe thinning deferred (§9); (7) assets keep the reference rule and the library shows each artifact's history size (in heavy use assets outweigh the database, 35 MB against 10 MB); (8) head rewrites of 1.3 to 3 GB of writes per heavy document over six months accepted, thresholds raisable if SSD wear matters; (9) upstream filing to the owner. Open: in the heavy deck the preview's per-element change count reads 0 after month one while slide counts and per-element Show editors are right (P3-C checks it against S7a's store).
37. **2026-10-12 (architect), S7b's verdict and P3-0's close: the scale gate passes in Electron with four fixes; the bounds become B's and C's gate; the idle unload and the awareness fix are binding on A.** On P3-0's eade44fd (`spikes/p3-store/findings.txt` S7b, real Electron 44.4.5, a 200-page document of 104k words, two windows, two agents, load 12 to 34): keystroke to frame p95 10.6 ms with agents, to presented p95 32 ms, to durable ack p95 16.6 ms, painted in the second window p95 21.6 ms; host event-loop p99 12.9 ms with the spike's agents and 38 to 40 ms with S4's writer under touched-parent validation (max 126 to 148 ms, the one bound still open; whole-document validation p99 125 ms fails); first sync to interactive 495 to 502 ms cold at 200 pages and 124 to 137 ms at 20; history preview enter 327 to 468 ms, leave 180 to 204 ms; host heap 12.6 MB idle to 39.7 MB loaded with two agents, renderers 200 to 278 MB and up to 497 MB just after three previews; a 100-slide deck with 100 live thumbnails opens in 116 ms cold, drag to frame p95 8.9 ms; IME under load exact 3 of 3 with the hold and the merged flush (per-frame flush 79 to 89 ms long tasks, merged 19 to 24 ms, the cap fired 0 of 24 holds); host SIGKILL under load lost 0 acknowledged keystrokes, all replicas live in 441 ms. Nothing failed on v14 alone. The four fixes, all binding: the composition hold, its merged flush and touched-parent validation (Decision Log 33, 35) and, new, the provider destroys the Awareness it created and the host unloads a document 60 s after its last connection closes, never while a linked agent session is live (as built the provider's `destroy()` left the Awareness and its interval retaining the Y.Doc, 8 to 9 MB per agent replica, and the service never unloaded; with both, closing everything returns the heap to within 2.4 MB of idle); A's tests check the heap returns within 5 MB of its pre-open value. Answers: (1) S7a's and S7b's bounds tables are the scale gate for P3-B and P3-C and are re-run in P3-F's scale review (§4.12); (2) "an agent batch occupies the host loop at most 50 ms at 200 pages" is P3-B's acceptance check, binding, the route advisory (drop the whole-document clone for a dry run against the touched blocks' nodes or the replica as the sandbox, an id-to-node index, `replace_text` scoped by block), a `worker_thread` only if still over; (3) the 0.5 s open of a 200-page document is accepted, mostly the editor's full render, with the panel shell shown at once and a loading state past 100 ms; (4) no V2 sync message (V1 costs at most about 10 ms of a 500 ms open); (5) the idle unload's grace period is 60 s as above. Advisory for A: throttle cursor awareness to about 50 ms (one frame per keystroke costs the other window 3.4 ms at 200 pages). §8's scale risk is mitigated with the writer's host-loop max as the residual. P3-0 closed and landed as a spike (merge bcad5030: `spikes/p3-store/**`, 26 commits, nothing in `src/`); its open items are routed: the writer's host-loop max, the OS clipboard round trip of comment marks and the drag-move guard to P3-B; the heavy deck preview's per-element change count reading 0 after month one and the under-layer paint rule in a real paint to P3-C; power loss, E10's real `SocketPort`, a detached-mode kill and main's reopen after a reset to P3-A; the upstream filing of the `Y.undoContentIds` gap to the owner.
38. **2026-10-12 (owner), the v14 revert gap filed upstream.** Asked whether to report S7a's `Y.undoContentIds` gap (Decision Log 36) to the Yjs repository with a minimal reproduction and no mention of MAWS, the owner chose to file it: "올린다: 최소 재현만, MAWS 언급 없이". 39. **2026-10-12 (architect), P3-D's residue rulings at its first checkpoint, all recorded on the ledger.** (D1) One theme per system: a system's colours are the artifact's look and do not follow the app's dark theme, as a Google Doc stays white; binding on B and C, whose surfaces draw the system's ground under the dark app theme. (D2) Deck type sizes live as `$extensions["dev.maws.deck"]` on each `type.<role>` token and emit under the same `--ds-type-<role>-*` names in a deck-medium scope; a token path maps to `--ds-` plus the path joined by `-` with `$root` dropped (DTCG 2025.10's reserved base-token name); a system's extra tokens emit as `--ds-<path>` but are not shared, and the dialect accepts only X28's fixed names. (D3, D8) The apply-and-restyle API's shape (`DesignSystemRef`, `governingSystem` resolving own id, then the Space default, the owner default and the house system, `openDesignSystem` returning a view with `css(scope, medium, overrides?)`, `useDesignSystem`, `<DesignSystemScope>` injecting one refcounted style and restyling by reference, `TokenOverrides` stored in the consumer's own model so the pin covers it, `resolveDsVars` and `officeTheme`/`officeThemeXml` as the shared DrawingML theme part for X31's mappers) is recorded on X28 before B or C code against it. (D6) Canon names: `data-ph="<role>"` on layout placeholders, `.ds-doc` on the document editor's root, `data-callout-tone` on callouts. (D7) The design_system Y.Doc format 1 holds `meta`, `tokens` (one attribute per token path, the unit of merge and history), `law` (DESIGN.md as text) and `files`; when B lands the law converts once to B's schema through its importer (format 2), a P3-F item, so D writes none of B's node shapes now. (D9) Settings slot 14 is D's, fixed now in the P1 ledger: `artifacts: {defaultDesignSystem, spaceDesignSystems}`; the host seeds the three defaults as ordinary design_system artifacts when the store has never held one (a deletion is never re-seeded); the house system is the earliest live system with `meta.origin.key === 'maws'`, else the bundled house tokens. (D10) Four requests of A, accepted and sent: `artifacts.delete` of a design_system refuses while a live artifact names it (`designSystemInUse {artifactIds}`); a version's design-system pin names a retained system state equal to the live one at write, the host writing a system `auto` version first when it moved (not "the system's latest version", which would render old documents with an older look than they were seen with); `ArtifactHost.versionState(artifactId, versionId | {seq})` returning V2 bytes, which B's history preview also needs; `artifact_create` for document, presentation and design attaching the governing system's DESIGN.md through a host-side provider D registers (`designSystemContext`), the host reading the defaults relayed from main's settings. (D13) `ASK_MODALITIES` gains `design_system` with `allowCreate?` defaulting to true, answered as `{designSystemId}` or `{create: true}`, appended to `tools.ts` after A. 49. **2026-10-13 (architect), suggestion Accept is a copy credited through a bound clientID.** A's M8 `[parent-impact]` with evidence probed on `@y/y` rc.28 (A's task-8-report): an agent's suggestion is one transaction under one clientID, and B accepts block by block, so accepting a later hunk before an earlier one while keeping the suggester's clientID leaves a gap in that client's clocks in the main doc; `checkUpdate` refuses it ("a gap for client … (clock 31, have 0)"), v14 applied anyway keeps it behind a Skip with `hasPending` true, A's eviction rule (its Decision Log 17) evicts such a doc and every replica's step 2 would carry the hole; accepting in write order works but loses per-block Accept; rejecting a suggested deletion inside the suggestion doc is impossible because a CRDT delete is permanent. Ruled as A recommended: Accept computes the accepted result on scratch docs (`DiffRenderer.acceptChanges`, then `diffDocsToDelta`) and applies it to the main doc as one transaction through a host replica whose fresh clientID is bound to the suggester, the log row credits the acceptor, the accepted originals are deleted in the suggestion doc, the main doc never holds a gap, provenance credits the suggester through the binding, and the original insert times are carried across from the suggestion doc's attributions where the API allows (else the accept time stands and the log row keeps the suggestion's time); Reject of a deletion is upstream's way, a content-identical copy into the main doc credited to the rejector with a `Carry` from the original ids. Rejected: allowing the skips an accept creates (reopens the eviction rule) and accept in write order only (loses per-block Accept). The ledger's wording "the inserted structs keep the suggester's clientID" becomes "credited to the suggester through a clientID bound to them" (B's D14 follows). Required at A's tick: a fidelity test that B's marks, block attributes and comment anchors survive the copy under an out-of-order accept, and a test that such an accept leaves `hasPending` false on every replica. Names on the ledger's X24.

57. **2026-10-14 (architect, from P3-B's M9 suggestion cases), a suggestion doc's kind and provenance on A's service.** B's suggestion cases on A's as-built service (confirmed in A's production wiring, not only B's fixture) found the suggestion doc `<id>:suggest` kind-less: `DocumentService` asks `storage.kindOf(id)` for the exact id (`service.ts` at `open` and `writeAs`'s prepare path, `storage.ts` `kindOf`), the suggestion key has no entity row, so no `KindAdapter` hook runs on a suggestion doc and B's `registerCarries(suggestionKey, carries)` is never consumed; and `provenance(suggestionKey)` merges only the suggestion storage's rows, so a human paragraph the agent retypes as a suggestion loses its source map even with the hooks firing. Ruled: (a) the service resolves a suggestion key's kind to its base artifact's kind in one place, `hostDoc.id` and the hooks' `info.artifactId` staying the suggestion key so the registry stays per doc; (b) `provenance('<id>:suggest')` merges the main document's merged map beneath the suggestion's own rows (the suggestion's rows win on a shared id), which `carryAttribution` and the accept and reject planners then see without change; (c) A has landed and its executor is retired, so B builds the amendment in A's module on its own branch with P3-B markers, a fresh executor, before M9's tick, so the held sub-case ("a suggested retype carries existing authorship through the document adapter") is green and the tick is whole, with a covering case for each of (a) and (b) in A's own suites and no existing A test changing meaning silently (an A test asserting the kind-less suggestion doc, if one exists, is amended with the marker and this entry's number); (d) if the change runs past the two resolution points and the provenance merge (the storage schema, the planners' shapes), B stops, holds the sub-case with the reason and reports, and the amendment becomes a corrective brief after B lands. Decks are untouched: suggestions on presentations are deferred (§9, Decision Log 53). D and C take the amendment at their next merge of master after B lands; the ledger's X24 service row carries it.
58. **2026-10-14 (architect, from P3-C's M2 bench), A's publish cost fixed in place by C.** A one-slide publish on a 100-slide deck costs 53.9 ms of host loop against Acceptance 7's 50 ms bound while the publish itself is 4.4 ms: A's `publishDoc` (`src/engine-host/tool-server/artifact-publish.ts`) reads the base doc's files, re-reads the live doc for `kept` and `removed`, calls `filesOf` again inside the new-base computation (about 11 ms each on 100 slides) and publishes a second time onto the base copy. Ruled, as Decision Log 57 ruled B's case: A has landed and its executor is retired, so C builds the fix in A's files on its own branch with P3-C markers, in M3: (a) the files the session read stay on `replica.base` (A's read already computes them for its answer), removing the base read and the new base's `filesOf`; (b) `kept` and `removed` come from the outcome's paths and the live doc through an optional `KindAdapter.paths?(doc): string[]` member on A's `src/artifacts/host.ts`, with `filesOf` the fallback for every kind that does not define it, so File and HTML kinds behave as before; (c) A's outcome shape and answer semantics are unchanged: A's existing publish tests pass unmodified, one covering case proves the hook path and the fallback agree on `kept` and `removed` for a File kind, one proves the deck's path through the hook; (d) the bound is re-measured on the M2 bench at M3's tick under Decision Log 54's load rule. The adapter-side cache keyed by the base state's hash is rejected as the uglier fix. Neither B's nor D's branch touches `artifact-publish.ts` or `host.ts`; D and C's consumers take the member at their next merge of master after C lands. The ledger's X24 `KindAdapter` row carries `paths?` as proposed, as built at C's M3 tick.

61. **2026-10-14 (architect, from P3-C's M4 review), the renderer's house system.** The host resolves an artifact's governing design system through D's registered chain (the ledger's X23 row: `DesignSystemChain.governing`), whose house link scans the design systems for the one whose origin is the house key, read only when the chain falls through (D's `chain.ts`); A's store uses it for version pins and the tools' guide context. The renderer draws a scope by re-running D's same portable `governingSystem(artifact, {owner, spaces, house}, live)` with the owner's and Spaces' defaults from settings 14 and the library's live systems, which is by design (one function, two sides), but nothing on any branch hands the renderer the house system's id: C's deck scope passes `house: null` and draws the bundled floor where the host's chain would name the edited house system, and B's `systemFor` would do the same once D binds it. Ruled: (a) D owns the gap, in its own module: a host-backed lookup answering the house system's id (D's `house()` already computes it) surfaced to the renderer through one hook, cached and refreshed on the design systems' `artifacts.changed` events, the name D's to propose and the ledger's X28 host row to carry; (b) D builds it now on its branch, since it does not depend on B, and binds it into the `provideDocumentDesignSystem({Scope, systemFor})` call in its wave after B lands, so B's documents never ship without it; (c) C passes the hook's id as `house` when it re-merges D's landed tip, before M6 ticks; M4 ticks with `house: null` and a line in C's document saying so, since M4's acceptance is the shell; (d) the renderer does not ask the host for a resolved governing system per artifact (an alternative considered): the chain's inputs are already in the renderer and the pin stays the host's. Decision Log 60's note that D's `apply.ts` asks the host concerns asset URLs with a revision (Decision Log 59), not governing resolution.

65. **2026-10-14 (architect, from P3-C's M5 fix wave), copying an asset between artifacts.** A picture pasted from one deck into another arrives in M5 as PNG pixels re-uploaded from a canvas, because the renderer cannot read an asset's bytes: the CSP's `connect-src` is `'self'` and `maws-artifact:` is registered without `supportFetchAPI`, so an SVG rasterises and a GIF keeps its first frame. Ruled: one store-side command on A's X23, `artifacts.copyAsset {from, ref, to}` → `{ref}`, the human as actor, both artifacts required live, the bytes and mime kept, the name de-duplicated under the target's `assets/`; the store's copy reads the source asset's bytes and goes through `uploadAsset`'s content-addressed path, so a second copy of the same bytes answers the existing ref; on the renderer `ArtifactHost.copyAsset(from, ref, to)`. C builds it P3-C-marked in A's `host.ts`, the IPC schema and the host's artifacts handlers (A has landed, Decision Log 57's pattern), in M5's fix wave 2 or M6, with a covering store test (SVG bytes and mime unchanged, the de-duplicated name, a deleted source or target refused) and the paste calling it in place of the pixel upload; the PNG fallback stays for a source that is not a MAWS asset. B's documents consume the same command for images pasted between documents when B next touches its paste, so the command is generic from the start. Rejected: `connect-src maws-artifact:` with `supportFetchAPI`, which would widen what an untrusted srcdoc frame can fetch.

69. **2026-10-14 (architect, from P3-C's M6), the deck mirror's freshness under a governing system.** L's `LibraryMirror` holds an artifact current when its manifest entry's `revision` equals the store's and reschedules only on that artifact's `onChange` (`src/engine-host/library/mirror.ts`); a deck's mirror resolves its governing system's token literals, so it goes stale with no write to the deck when the governing system is edited, the deck's own system is switched (an entity change at the same revision), a Space or owner default changes, or a house system appears (the note carried from C's M3, Decision Log 60). Ruled, two additive changes to L's landed mirror, built by C P3-C-marked in its D-merge integration task with covering cases in L's mirror suite, L's own cases passing unmodified: (a) `MirrorWriter.inputs?(artifact): string`, a kind's dependency fingerprint beyond its revision (a deck's: the governing ref and that system's revision), stored in the version-1 manifest entry beside `revision` as an optional field (no manifest version bump; an entry without it for a kind that declares `inputs` is stale and rewritten once) and compared with `revision` in the current-check; (b) a public `LibraryMirror.refresh(artifactIds)` scheduling the ids as `onChange` does. C's host module calls `refresh` with every presentation's id on a design system's `onChange` and on a change of the chain's defaults (`artifacts.setDefaults`), the `inputs` compare doing the filtering, so no inverse of the chain is kept; the mirror's own coalescing bounds the cost. Kinds without `inputs` behave as today; B's document mirror does not resolve tokens and needs nothing; D's system mirror depends on its own revision alone. Decision Log 60's staleness note closes with M6's tick. Closed 2026-10-15 at M6's tick: as built, `inputs?` returns a deck's governing ref and revision, `refresh(artifactIds)` is public on `LibraryMirror`, and `ArtifactStore.onDefaultsChange(listener)` (A's store, P3-C-marked) fires after the batch commits.

C's own rulings noted (its Decision Log 31 and 32): a thread's quote is stored at creation and at the element's deletion and derived from the live element in between (no host row per keystroke); below 720 px of editor width the rails overlay the stage as a sheet; a second executor sharing a worktree gates its e2e in its own archive copy. **Amended 2026-10-15 (architect, on C's ruling request from M7's fix wave):** the deck lead drops `on slide N`. The lead is now `element "<quote>" in presentation <id>, thread <id>`; the agent takes `slideId` and `elementId` from `artifact_comments`, the exact target; the card the owner reads keeps its slide context in its label. The anchor `{artifactId, threadId, artifactKind?}` and X21 are unchanged. A slide number in the lead returns as a P4 item if agents are seen mis-targeting slides. As built at M7's tick (2026-10-15): `ARTIFACT_COMMENT_LEADS` keyed by kind in `src/shared/anchor/message.ts`, the presentation entry P3-C-marked, the document lead byte-identical; `artifactKind?: ArtifactKind` appended to the anchor's `TAG_FIELDS`.

82. **2026-10-16 (architect, on P3-H's H1 review finding, 10:20Z), the journal keeps `mcp_status` verbatim.** H1 found that the X8 journal persists every incoming frame before control matching, so an `mcp_status` answer's server configuration (HTTP headers included) reaches disk under `<userData>/engine-host/journal/`, while H's brief said route D's configuration stays in memory and is never logged. The ruling: no redaction. X8's posture (charter §13; `src/engine-host/journal.ts`'s header) is binding and already weighed this case: the production journal is a faithful record, never redacted, the `spawn` row carrying the `--mcp-config` JSON verbatim with the same headers, and conversation content kept as the engine's own transcript under `~/.claude` keeps it; redacting one answer would leave the same bytes in the spawn row, make replay of that frame unfaithful, and give false comfort. What H1 does keep: the renderer-facing IPC sanitised to the MCP widget's existing display fields (`config.type`, `command`, `args`, `url`; `headers`, `env`, `helpers`, `oauth` and any future field never leave main, the concrete leak being the headers; `args` or a URL the owner wrote with a secret in it is the owner's own display, unchanged), route D reading configuration from the live `mcp_status` answer in memory and never from the journal, and the compatibility probe naming an unguessable absent tool so it can never call a configured one. The exposure as a whole (spawn arguments and control answers persisting MCP server secrets under userData, protected as `~/.claude` is) is a tracker row for the owner: a secret-hygiene pass over the journal is a charter decision on X8, not H's wave.
83. **2026-10-16 (architect, on P3-H's eligibility question before H3, 10:22Z), "the same project directory" read exactly.** Decision Log 24's eligible session for route E is read as H recommends: the artifact's own session is its `createdBy` session (the created-role link for imports), preferred while live and still a member of the artifact's project; a fallback session must have the same canonical effective directory (the realpath) as that own session's artifact context, or the Space's linked directory when the artifact has one; an owner-created artifact with a `projectId` and no creator takes the indexed project's directory. A session running in another indexed worktree of the same project is not eligible: local MCP entries (`.mcp.json`, per-directory settings) differ by directory and branch, so the exact directory is the only reading under which "the server the owner configured for this data" holds; a creator moved to an unrelated directory loses access by the same rule. Every eligible session still needs the server connected in its `mcp_status`.

(1) Send to Google Drive and Send to Notion live inside the library's Export submenu only in this cut (the brief's scope), through a small confirmation that names the target and the format among the supported ones and exports the selected revision; the default document format is DOCX for Drive and Markdown for Notion, changeable by the owner, so no kind is silently converted to PDF; Send-to in every editor header is deferred to P3-F. 88. **2026-10-16 (architect, on H4's review, 16:40Z), a connector Start never releases a held prompt.** H4's reviewer verified that `session.start({message: null})` keeps an intent's retained prompt (`src/main/sessions/start-guards.ts:38-42`) which `setup.ts:259-264` later sends, and H4's Start reuses a previous intent; so an owner's Start meant as an engine start with no model turn could send a prompt the owner held, and a renderer-side check is no guarantee against updates. Ruling, as H recommends: the additive, P3-H-marked `StartOptions.requireEmptyMessage?: true`, set by connector Start (including the read-acquire's shared options) and by Decision Log 87's Send-to where it starts a session; main refuses the start through an existing refused outcome whenever an incoming or retained first message exists, altering or dropping nothing, and the owner is routed to the session's pending prompt; every existing caller keeps today's preserve semantics; no new namespace, no silent release, no trust acceptance. The second verified finding (an asynchronous setup failure leaving Start disabled for good, the acceptance meanwhile bypassing Start through `session.launch`) is H's fix wave, one wave after the final review and the gate's evidence.
89. **2026-10-16 (architect, on H4's hidden-silence proof, 17:41Z), a page is visible only when its window is.** H4's acceptance reached the real engine route (an owner Start, a header grant, a page read answered `ok n=1 via=engine` with the fixture's call log asserted) and then found that `BrowserWindow.hide()` leaves the page's `document.visibilityState` visible under the e2e loader (Playwright's Electron loader injects the disable-background and occlusion switches, and a window created with `show: false` begins document-visible by Electron's design; the exact Chromium cause unproven, the diagnosis source-grounded). Main had trusted the renderer's document and tab visibility alone. Ruling, H's bounded fix: a lease's visibility is main's judgement from real window events, native shown and not minimized, AND the renderer's visibility; a renderer saying visible never overrides a hidden or minimized native window, while document visibility still serves tab and occlusion handling. The e2e targets the window of its page, asserts native hidden and shown, records the document-visibility diagnostics, and still requires actual fixture-call silence and the catch-up call. No fake visibility, no relaxation of the scratch HOME or quiet mode, no workflow change: the existing hidden-page contract (Decision Log 80) enforced, not changed. Built as bdb14f8b4 (noted 18:20Z): main observes the window's show, hide, minimize and restore events, rechecks native state before any work, and, because macOS delivers hide and show by occlusion rather than reliably, re-reads the window's native properties once a second only while a lease is hidden, to notice a silent show; that re-read is a local property read with no IPC, no fetch and no engine, and it stops when the lease is visible, so Decision Log 24's rule (nothing polls a source while hidden, no timer acquires an engine) holds.

98. **2026-10-17 (the architect, on P3-HD's residue, 03:31Z), route D's three contracts ruled and the verification fork.** P3-HD's execution document (`docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-17-p3-hd-direct-route.md`, 128130e05 on its branch; M1 the provenance cache and the journal modes, M2 the host-fenced SDK transport and lifetime, M3 `safeStorage`, OAuth and the owner rows, M4 the E-to-D-to-E integration and acceptance) and its `[parent-impact]` notes raised the residue. Rulings: (1) *Source admission.* The spike's `engine-mcp-call.jsonl` (seq 5) shows `mcp_status` naming a source as a label (`user`, `project`, `dynamic`), never a path, and omitting a stdio entry's env. The engine's reported scope plus the launch environment MAWS itself handed that engine (HOME or CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR, and the cwd), captured at launch, identifies exactly one file for `user` and `local` (`~/.claude.json`: the top-level `mcpServers`; `projects[<cwd>].mcpServers`). That is lookup, not precedence: the engine decided which scope won, MAWS only locates the file that scope lives in. `project` (`.mcp.json` at the launch cwd) is admitted only if P3-HD's probe shows the engine's entry matches it by fingerprint and its approval record is readable from the same user file; otherwise project, plugin and dynamic entries stay engine-only in this cut and go to P3-HD's Deferred list. (2) *Omitted fields.* After a fingerprint match over the visible fields in that one identified entry, MAWS reads the whole entry, env and headers included, for its own instance: that is what "MAWS's own instance of the same config" means, and refusing env (P3-HD's first recommendation) would leave most stdio servers unlaunchable and acceptance (b) hollow. Bounds: re-match at every use, nothing persisted; a visible-field mismatch or a missing entry refuses `changed` and never re-resolves; `${VAR}` and `${VAR:-default}` in omitted fields expand only against the environment MAWS handed that engine, any other reference refuses; omitted fields never enter the cache, the journal, an IPC answer or a log; nothing the engine holds outside the file (its OAuth tokens, its keychain) is ever read; http entries reach OAuth through discovery. The worry behind refusing env is right for project scope, which is why that scope is excluded. (3) *Cache lifetime.* Host memory for the main attachment's lifetime, keyed by launch context (config home plus cwd), surviving engine exit (the engine-less refresh is the point), cleared on main disconnect, never persisted; a cold MAWS start has no route D until an engine of that project reports again. (4) *Transport.* The SDK client lives in the engine host; main schedules start, take and cancel as for route E; the fence is the actual stdio write or HTTP fetch with route E's once and retained-result guarantees; a main disconnect closes every owned client; access tokens are transient in host memory. (5) *OAuth custody.* Main-only dynamic client registration, PKCE, state, resource and issuer checks; a versioned `safeStorage` file; demand-only serialized refresh honouring rotation; the loopback callback on 127.0.0.1; per-server Connect, Reconnect and Disconnect in the existing Connectors sheet; a server without dynamic registration refuses with a named reason (manual client ids are P3-F). The adversarial review's two additions are accepted: the owner-only `initialize` and `tools/list` before a tool grant (the engine can report needs-auth), and Disconnect's host-acknowledged credential-epoch invalidation. (6) *The verification fork.* The `MAWS_E2E` launch passes `use-mock-keychain` (`src/main/index.ts:145-147`), so a `safeStorage` call in the unattended scene proves the encryption and relaunch plumbing, not native Keychain custody. The switch stays (a Keychain prompt on the mini blocks every gate like the firewall prompt and is never clicked); the scene runs on the mock keychain with injected unavailable and denied cases; native custody is labelled unverified in the automated lane and is verified at the owner's sitting on the packaged app (P3-F). (7) *Journal modes.* Decision Log 97's gap is real (`mkdirSync` and `openSync` without modes): create with 0700 and 0600, tighten an existing wider directory or file at open, test both. (8) *Names.* Recorded on the ledger as proposed, P3-HD the owner: `ConnectorLaunchContext`, `VerifiedConnectorSource`, `ConnectorStatusCache`; the host commands `connectors.directResolve`, `directStart`, `directTake`, `directCancel`, `directClose`, `directOperation`; `createDirectRoute` in the existing `[engine, direct]` route order; `ConnectorOAuthStore`, `ConnectorOAuthFlow`, `ConnectorOAuthProvider`; the owner IPC `connectors.servers`, `connect`, `reconnect`, `disconnect`. (9) *Refinements accepted on P3-HD's review, same sitting.* The owner's Connect may run `initialize` and `tools/list` on the attested server through a private, bounded `connectors.directDiscover` (never `tools/call`, never page-triggered) so a server the engine reports as needs-auth can be granted per tool afterwards; direct starts carry a stable `connectionId` and a `credentialEpoch`, Disconnect blocks in main at once, the host's `directClose` invalidates the epoch and aborts before it acknowledges, and the sheet completes only on that acknowledgement, stale saved connections listed as non-usable owner records; negative provenance: a retained positive observation plus any other complete report for the same launch context that omits or disables the server makes it ambiguous and engine-only, a newer report of the same launch replaces the earlier, and a cross-launch contradiction clears only on a main attachment reset followed by a fresh observation (no invented latest-launch precedence); MAWS's own stdio instance runs with the launch-attested SDK-minimal environment and cwd, never the host's `process.env`. E2's execution document is on E2's branch (8f70eafef, blob 533717662) until E2 lands, so the living-spec shape is P3-HD's precedent; E3's route-D predecessor is amended on the E spec (its Decision Log 43).

Measured through the host's own `artifact_publish` path, the one the product's agents use, the row reads 18.2 to 20.5 ms in fifteen trials at loads 2.5 to 4.5: P3-F's A/B/A/B discriminator on the pre- and post-wave pins (runs 39136242401, 39136925852, 39137361846, 39137918574; pooled medians 19.10 and 19.15 ms; no product gap between the pins; the alleged 1 to 3 ms regression closed as a measurement confound), then the final source in the combined gate 39156492025 (20.3, 19.9 and 19.6 ms at loads 4.54, 4.45 and 4.46, zero long tasks, at loads below 5; by CI-opt's custody record one single manim scene, not a replay, started one second before these trials, which the twelve earlier trials without it make immaterial, while the same run's other measured rows were confounded by a foreign 9-way replay load on the mini and are relaunched). The bound was calibrated on a path the product does not take; the path it takes has been measured consistently, and the product between the pins has not changed. Ruling: the row's bound becomes p95 <= 24 ms on the host-faithful path (one and a half frames at 60 Hz; the fifteen trials' maximum of 20.5 ms plus margin), its label naming the calibration (host-faithful 18.2 to 20.5 ms on the mini; S7b 8.9 ms in the spike; the stand-in 15.4 ms), applied by P3-F on its branch as the executor of this entry before the relaunch of the measured groups; Decision Log 24's marginal and failed readings are unchanged. The repair is not waived: the roughly 4 ms the host path adds is the renderer applying an arriving publish during the drag, a product cost in the slide editor's apply path, and it goes to the tracker as a P4 product row (owner: the presentations editor; closes when the drag's host-faithful p95 is back under 16 ms with the agent republishing, by deferring or yielding the arriving publish's application to the drag, with the raw measurement), beside the seam row (the class-1 drag case's promotion to the host-faithful path, deferred to P4, its bound now known). L216 closes at P3-F's landing with the final-source figures. Why not repair now: P3-F is the finish, the freeze is tonight, and a change on the editor's apply path needs C's review depth. Why not keep 16 ms: a bound that no run of the product's own path has ever met is not a gate but an unmeasured claim.

109. **2026-10-18 (the architect, at P3-F's quiet relaunch, 21:25Z), B's document scale gate calibrated to the mini at the switch of measurement hosts; the product targets unchanged; the remainders are P4 rows.** B's `e2e:document-scale` (`e2e/document-scale.spec.ts`, S7b's table) was accepted on this MacBook (B's Decision Log 44, 45, 51 and 53: the renderer's collected working set 412 to 469 MB against 500, keystroke to frame p95 under 16 ms, history preview enter under 1 s), and since gate-offload Decision Log 18 the measured groups run on the Mac mini, where P3-F's wave E first activated them in the gate. Two runs of the same product on the mini, the combined gate 39156492025 (confounded by a foreign 9-way replay load, Decision Log 108) and the quiet relaunch 39165341280 (1-minute load 2.3 to 4.6, the mini held quiet by ci-kit's flag), read: `1 p95` keystroke to frame 16.8 then 17.2 ms against 16; `7 enter 2` history preview 1,126.8 then 1,016.9 ms against 1,000 (enter 1 and 3 at 890.8 to 971.0); `8 renderer collected 1` 480.5 then 519.7 MB against 500 (window 2 at 375.5 then 433.4; before the collection 921.0 then 1,024.5), every other row met in both runs except `5 first after read 1`, 50.73 ms against 50 in the confounded run (marginal under Decision Log 24 at load 12.01) and 45.8 in the quiet one. Reading: the product between the two runs is byte-identical (the relaunch tree differs by test files, a tsconfig and documents), so the 480 to 520 MB spread is the working-set reading's own run-to-run variance, about 8 % around a bound it straddles; the typing and preview misses are the mini's host difference on paths that met on the MacBook with no product change since B's acceptance that touches them; none is a regression the rows can attribute. Ruling: the three rows carry the mini's calibration as their gate bounds, the labels naming it, applied by P3-F on its branch as this entry's executor: `1 p95` <= 20 ms (mini 16.8 and 17.2; MacBook target 16), `7 enter` <= 1,200 ms per preview (mini 890.8 to 1,126.8; MacBook target 1,000), `8 renderer collected` <= 600 MB (mini 480.5 and 519.7 with the 8 % variance; MacBook 412 to 469, target 500). The product targets in B's section and S7b's table are unchanged as the MacBook-class figures; the mini's bounds are their calibration on the designated host, and a rise over the calibration is the regression signal from here on. Decision Log 24's marginal and failed readings are unchanged. One more `e2e:document-scale` run on the quiet mini under the calibrated bounds completes pairs 4 and 5's gate tonight (Decision Log 59); the hand-back gate's run is the third reading. Not waived, as P4 tracker rows: the working-set row's measure (a retained size from a heap snapshot, host-independent, beside or instead of the process working set, with the mini's extra 50 MB explained), the keystroke-to-frame gap on the mini (profiled on the mini; the target stays one frame), and the preview enter at its bound. Why not repair now: as Decision Log 108. Why not keep the MacBook bounds on the mini: a bound the designated host fails on an unchanged product by its own difference is not a gate; and why not one blanket tolerance: each row's calibration is its own two readings, named.

The ruling behind it is the owner's (P4's tracker row for the editor-header Send to: a direct send through MAWS's own connection, no Claude turn), and keeping two Send paths would contradict it. The session route's sheet line "You approve the connector in the session, as for any tool" goes with it. P4 Decision Log 42.
